# Security invariants

State privacy intent separately from enforcement. Check reports proved, refuted
with a replayed counterexample, or unproved with a reason; passing finite tests
are not proofs.

```quarry
table privateNotes:
  patient: principal
  body: text 1..2000
  read <- .patient = viewer
  insert <- .patient = viewer
  online only
invariant privateNotes.read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.body read only .patient = viewer
invariant privateNotes.insert only .patient = viewer
query privateNote(note: privateNotes):
  return privateNotes[note]
mutation writePrivateNote(body: text 1..2000):
  return insert privateNotes { patient: viewer, body }
```

Run `snapback4 check`, `snapback4 audit` (or `check --audit`), then
`snapback4 test`. `audit --json` lists conditional row predicates, operations,
columns, inherited grants and grant writers. Its final section lists conditional role/row/effect exposures with no matching invariant; declaring one effect does not cover the others. `check` names broad signed-in
reads and missing intent, once per check for the project. Review `snapback/generated/security-invariants.q.draft` against the specification; it mirrors existing rules and can repeat the same mistakes. Confirm or correct each candidate and copy chosen declarations into an authored `.q` file. The draft is never compiled or auto-accepted. The test command runs isolated schema-derived
challenges automatically. Replayed authored observations from trusted fixtures fail acceptance; seed-only and synthetic witnesses carry separate reachability labels, remain in JSON, and are shown in text with `--all`. They do not fail check/test. Coverage
that needs a live HTTP server, fixture assets, operation sequences or a device
is named as not covered; zero violations alone does not qualify an app secure.

## Reference

`invariant table.column read only P` protects a column value, derived values and value-dependent counts/existence/refusals. An authored projection excluding the column does not by itself disclose that value; filtering, ordering or copying it can. Audit JSON includes `column_audiences` (effective row rule plus private-owner gate). Unknown derived and opaque paths remain unproved.

`invariant table.read only P` protects read intent; insert, update and delete
name authority. `invariant table.column write only P` covers writes to a
column (both insert and update). `invariant table always P` states row integrity.
An update policy over `.field` constrains **both old and next images**.
A policy saying status must not be override therefore also restricts editing
a row that was already override, even when changing it away from that status.
For the role authority of one named mutation, use operation-specific intent
(`invariant table.operation only P`) instead. General next-image-only and
cross-operation transition assertions still need a separate reduction.
Predicates use the existing rule vocabulary: `.field`, `viewer`, `exists`,
comparisons, `and`, `or`, `not`. For a role write use the complete roster probe,
for example `invariant members.role write only exists roles[viewer, role = 'owner']`
in a schema declaring those tables. A declaration never grants authority.

The implemented proof fragment exhaustively checks independent three-valued
rule predicates. A failed implication is unproved until an engine replay
establishes a counterexample. A schema-valid seeded snapshot is not a claim
that an application operation sequence reaches that state. Required-column
non-null assertions have a separate validation theorem. Read/count/sum/existence obligations are reported separately. A maintained aggregate with bare privacy keys can prove its contributor audience; a broader aggregate can yield a paired present/absent counterexample. Exact server `not before`/`expire at` bounds can prove a temporal read component independently of physical sweeps. General transitions and unsupported expressions remain unproved.
Read-through captures/live parents, complex derived aggregates, raw rule probes,
owned-field update bypasses, cascade/set-null and opaque jobs/workers need
separate reductions. An unresolved path blocks the whole declaration's proof.

Server delivery authorizes against the current server snapshot. A disconnected
device can retain an earlier snapshot until it receives a revocation; even
`until-revoked` cannot know a later server role. Synced current-role intent is
therefore unproved for device access. Retained history follows its leave policy.
These reports exclude physical timing and resource/meter side channels and
explicitly exclude the trusted system-principal boundary. Service seats remain
inside the ordinary-viewer check; there is no implicit seat exemption.

## Current roles and derived grants

A membership or assignment created while a user is an inspector can survive
their demotion. Conjoin the **current reader's role** with the assignment
branch, or revoke every derived grant transactionally whenever the role changes.
A grant writer's role does not establish the recipient's current authority.
With independent current-role intent, grant-lifetime advice points at this pattern; the argument-grant advice identifies
arguments written into reader-grant columns: validate the chosen principal's
role and relationship, even when the caller is privileged.
Global admin authority must come from a trusted operator or existing admin;
a group-creation `created` probe must remain scoped to that group everywhere.
`N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION` identifies role assignments trusted by later rules without independent delegation. Open enrollment may be intentional: compare each note with the specification. See `guide staging` for operator first-admin setup and `guide sharing` for parent authority.

## Required evidence and deadlines

If the spec requires a photo, use a required `image <=5000000` column or
`[image <=5000000] 1..4`; `?` permits omission and `[image] <=4` permits zero.
Uploads are verified media and asset reads inherit row and private-field rules.
Optional-evidence advice asks the builder to check intentional optionality.
Text `min..max` counts non-whitespace Unicode characters toward the minimum;
the maximum counts all characters. Stored text is not trimmed or rewritten.
A sweep changes state eventually: compare the deadline with server `now` in
the mutation too if writes must stop exactly at that instant.

## Existence observations

A lookup that says NOT_FOUND on absence followed by a denial saying FORBIDDEN
can reveal existence to callers admitted by a broader read rule. Use the same
code **and message** for hidden/absent/denied cases when existence is private,
or tighten the table read rule. Program `exists` is caller-filtered; probes
inside table rules inspect raw rows and can disclose predicates indirectly.
Inspect both paths in `audit`; a payload-only read check is insufficient.

## When an observer remains unproved

The report names the edge that lacks a reduction. Preserve the specification
while changing its enforcement; weakening an invariant to match a leaking
rule does not repair privacy.

- Maintained counts/sums: retain privacy fields as ordinary bare group keys
  and protect the aggregate with the same audience predicate. Recorded
  reference paths, calendar keys, derived values, private columns and temporal
  contributors need their own reduction; their names are not source fields.
- Owner fields: declare column-write intent too. `written by` authorizes
  patches even when the ordinary update rule denies. To prove a particular
  mutation's role authority, use a dominating current-role requirement and
  operation-specific intent over a role table the mutation cannot change.
  This does not prove every owner-field or old/next-image path.
- Raw rule probes: inspect the observing table and its writers. A protected
  target's read rule does not protect predicates probed from another rule.
  Apply the intended audience at that observer, or remove the raw dependency;
  the report retains the unsupported edge until it can establish implication.
- Cascade/set-null: automatic child changes bypass child write rules. An
  explicit authorized child mutation with `restrict` removes that automatic
  writer, but parent refusal/existence and transitive references remain
  separate observations. Read-through/live-parent authority requires a separate reduction.
- Jobs/native workers/actions: the audit names the trusted or opaque writer.
  An ordinary mutation with stable role requirements can prove that mutation's
  authority; arbitrary native/worker output cannot acquire a proof from a
  caller's role or a passing finite test.
- Schedules: use a stored `not before`/`expire at` lifetime for exact read
  boundaries, or compare server `now` in the write itself. A scheduled status
  update leaves the old status visible between deadline and sweep.

Default `test` challenges pure-schema applications through a private production
`serve`: unauthenticated calls, forged persona headers and development doors.
The host uses no pre-existing developer credentials or development mode. Applications needing
native/worker artifacts are explicitly not covered by that schema-only host.
Focused fixtures also enable short ordered mutation histories followed by
ordinary reads; traces record actual calls and current policy evaluation.
Named personas/service seats and enum argument combinations are enumerated
within reported limits. Asset challenges use real foreign-upload and verified
non-image ledgers; video arguments and unvisited histories remain named. Complete fixture stores preserve image/file ledgers, captures, read-through deadlines, maintained views and expiry. Histories include fixture principals and reference-row candidates, under a reported work cap. Neither
these finite campaigns nor an offline device's retained snapshot establishes
current-server revocation at a disconnected access.

### E_SECURITY_TARGET

E_SECURITY_TARGET means the invariant target does not name a declared table, column or effect. Correct the target name; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SECURITY_INTENT

N_SECURITY_INTENT means access rules lack independent declared intent. Declare policy from the spec; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_BROAD_READ

N_BROAD_READ identifies universal or roster-wide readership; its message states the exact condition. Confirm the intended audience; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE

N_GRANT_OUTLIVES_ROLE means a stored grant may survive a later role change. Recheck the current reader role; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_ARGUMENT_GRANT

N_ARGUMENT_GRANT means an argument becomes a reader-grant column. Validate the recipient role and relationship; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE

N_OPTIONAL_EVIDENCE means optional media permits omitted evidence. Declare required media when the spec requires it; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE

N_SWEEP_ONLY_DEADLINE means sweeps leave time between a deadline and cleanup. Enforce the deadline in the mutation; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

### N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE

N_EXISTENCE_ORACLE means absence and later denial can reveal different outcomes. Unify the code and message when existence is private; inspect `audit` and the policy before changing a rule.

The isolated production HTTP campaign also signs in, refreshes the session,
logs out all sessions and retries both retired credentials against query,
mutation, schema, changes, sync, probe and refresh doors. It reports missing coverage
when ordinary identity or complete deployed worker artifacts are unavailable.
This checks server admission; a disconnected device can still retain old bytes.

### N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION

N_PRIVILEGE_ESCALATION identifies self-signup roles or role-grant operations that can confer authority trusted by other rules. Restrict assignment to authorized delegation; provision the first administrator with `guide staging`.

Identity rate refusals return HTTP `Retry-After` (seconds) and
`denied.retryAfterMs` (milliseconds), conservatively covering the 60-second
window. Wait that interval before retrying identity requests.

REL5 owns release transitions/old-client compatibility; MIG owns backfills and
staging clock control for day-two qualification.

//! Equal-image substitution only for engine-enforced immutable local fields.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#111-avoid-duplicate-concrete-searches — no reference or mutable-field equality is assumed.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::schema::{Table,Constraint};

pub(super) fn stable(table:&Table,rule:&RuleExpr)->RuleExpr{
    fn term(table:&Table,value:&mut RuleTerm){
        if let RuleTerm::OldField(field)=value && (field=="id"||table.constraints.iter().any(|c|matches!(c,Constraint::Immutable{fields} if fields.contains(field)))) {
            *value=RuleTerm::Field(field.clone());
        }
    }
    fn walk(table:&Table,rule:&mut RuleExpr){match rule{
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b)|RuleExpr::Ne(a,b)|RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b)=>{term(table,a);term(table,b);},
        RuleExpr::Exists{key,equalities,..}|RuleExpr::Created{key,equalities,..}=>{
            for value in key.iter_mut().chain(equalities.iter_mut().map(|(_,t)|t)){term(table,value);}
        },
        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for rule in xs{walk(table,rule)},
        RuleExpr::Not(rule)=>walk(table,rule),
        RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Deny|RuleExpr::Public{..}=>{},
    }}
    let mut out=rule.clone();walk(table,&mut out);out
}
pub(super) fn update(table:&Table,policy:&RuleExpr)->Result<bool,String>{
    kernel::implies(&stable(table,&table.update_rule_judged()),&stable(table,&policy.old_and_next()))
}
//! Conservative permission map; conditional predicates are never universal grants.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#5-exposure-report — include grant writers and implicit effects.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::ir;
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct Grant {pub table:String,pub predicate:String,pub writers:Vec<String>}
#[derive(Clone,Debug,Serialize)]
pub struct Exposure {pub table:String,pub columns:BTreeMap<String,String>,pub column_audiences:BTreeMap<String,String>,pub effects:BTreeMap<String,String>,pub operations:Vec<String>,pub paths:Vec<String>,pub grants:Vec<Grant>}
pub(super) fn mentions(json:&serde_json::Value,table:&str)->bool {
    match json {serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>fields.iter().any(|(k,v)| k=="table"&&v.as_str()==Some(table)||mentions(v,table)),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|x|mentions(x,table)),_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn authored_refusal(json:&serde_json::Value)->bool {
    match json {serde_json::Value::Object(fields)=>fields.get("Require").is_some()||fields.values().any(authored_refusal),serde_json::Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(authored_refusal),_=>false}
}
fn writers(bound:&Bound,name:&str)->Vec<String> {
    let mut out=Vec::new();let t=&bound.schema.tables[name];
    for effect in [Effect::Insert,Effect::Update] {out.push(format!("{effect} rule: {}",t.rules.for_effect(effect).quarry()));}
    for p in &bound.programs {for (table,kind) in ir::write_tables(&p.program) {if table==name {out.push(format!("{} ({kind}, {}; {})",p.program.name,p.program.kind.word(),if p.program.kind==ir::ProgramKind::Job {"system boundary"}else{"caller rules/field authority"}));}}}
    for (column,authority) in &t.written_by {out.push(format!("column {column}: owner .{} can patch under field authority",authority.owner));}
    for m in &bound.schema.maintains {if m.target==name||m.private.as_ref().is_some_and(|p|p.table==name){out.push(format!("system maintain {} from {}",m.target,m.source));}}
    if !t.on_delete.is_empty(){out.push(format!("referential actions: {:?}",t.on_delete));}
    if bound.programs.iter().any(|p|p.program.native)||!bound.schema.action_specs.is_empty()||!bound.schema.effects.is_empty(){out.push("native/worker/external paths: opaque trusted writers (not an absence claim)".into());}
    out.push("operator/system seed, import, restore and activation (trusted boundary)".into());out.sort();out.dedup();out
}
pub(super) fn exposures(bound:&Bound)->Vec<Exposure> {
    bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(name,t)| {
        let columns=t.columns.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)).map(|(c,k)| {
            let authority=t.written_by.get(c).map(|a|format!("; written by .{}; {}",a.owner,if a.audience{"audience readable"}else{"private to owner"})).unwrap_or_default();
            (c.clone(),format!("{k}{authority}"))
        }).collect();
        let effects=[("read",t.rules.read.quarry()),("insert",t.rules.insert.quarry()),("update",t.update_rule_judged().quarry()),("delete",t.rules.delete.quarry())].into_iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.into(),v)).collect();
        let operations=bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|mentions(&serde_json::to_value(&p.program).unwrap(),name)).map(|p|format!("{} ({})",p.program.name,p.program.kind.word())).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let mut paths=vec!["server point/list/filter/view/search/count/exists/live-refetch: ordinary ruled reads".into(),"mutation valued write images: authorized final reread; logical refusals separately unproved".into()];
        if t.is_expiring()||t.not_before.is_some(){paths.push(format!("live rows only: not before {:?}, expire {:?}; server clock gates delivery",t.not_before,t.expire));}
        if let Some(sync)=&t.sync {paths.push(format!("sync/device envelope: {}; device access uses delivered snapshot; leave {:?}",sync.line(),t.leave));}
        if t.columns.values().any(|k|k.contains_asset()){paths.push("asset read: owning-row read rule AND applicable owner-private column authority; upload placement: insert/update authority".into());}
        if let Some(through)=&t.through {paths.push(format!("read through {}.{} -> {} (capture/live reduction unproved)",name,through.reference,through.parent));}
        for m in &bound.schema.maintains {if m.source==*name||m.target==*name {paths.push(format!("derived {} -> {} (independent aggregate audience)",m.source,if m.target.starts_with("$column:"){ "maintained column" }else{&m.target}));}}
        if !t.on_delete.is_empty(){paths.push(format!("incoming referential actions bypass child rules: {:?}",t.on_delete));}
        let mut grants=Vec::new();let mut pending=Vec::new();let mut visited=BTreeSet::new();
        for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete] {for dep in kernel::dependencies(r){pending.push((dep,r.quarry()));}}
        while let Some((dep,predicate))=pending.pop(){if !visited.insert(dep.clone()){continue;}let Some(g)=bound.schema.table(&dep)else{continue};
            grants.push(Grant{table:dep.clone(),predicate,writers:writers(bound,&dep)});
            for r in [&g.rules.read,&g.rules.insert,&g.rules.update,&g.rules.delete]{for next in kernel::dependencies(r){pending.push((next,r.quarry()));}}
        }
        let column_audiences=t.columns.keys().filter(|c|!t.engine_field(c)).map(|c|(c.clone(),super::columns::admission(t,Some(c)).quarry())).collect();
        Exposure{table:name.clone(),columns,column_audiences,effects,operations,paths,grants}
    }).collect()
}
/// Role labels retain complete predicates, including relationships. Every
/// entry is conditional; a roster's existence is never assumed for all users.
pub(super) fn roles(exposures:&[Exposure])->BTreeMap<String,Vec<String>> {
    let mut out:BTreeMap<String,Vec<String>>=BTreeMap::new();
    for x in exposures {for (effect,predicate) in &x.effects {
        if predicate=="deny"{continue;}
        let label=if predicate=="allow"||predicate=="public"||predicate.starts_with("public "){"every signed-in user".into()}else{format!("caller satisfying {predicate}")};
        out.entry(label).or_default().push(format!("{} {effect}: {predicate}; operations: {}",x.table,x.operations.join(", ")));
    }}out
}
//! Typed server-clock facts used only by the proof abstraction, never runtime rules.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#102-temporal-intent — lifetime is independent of physical sweep lag.
use super::*;
use snapback4_core::{Value, ir::BinaryOp as Op, schema::{Table, ColumnType}};
// Quarry identifiers cannot contain ':'. No authored field aliases this atom.
const CLOCK: &str = "$security:server-clock";
pub(super) fn clock() -> RuleTerm { RuleTerm::Field(CLOCK.into()) }
pub(super) fn has_clock(rule: &RuleExpr) -> bool {
    rule.any_term(&|t| matches!(t, RuleTerm::Field(f) if f == CLOCK))
}
pub(super) fn comparison(op: Op, a: RuleTerm, b: RuleTerm) -> RuleExpr {
    match op {
        Op::Gt => RuleExpr::Compare(Op::Lt,b,a), Op::Gte => RuleExpr::Compare(Op::Lte,b,a),
        Op::Eq => RuleExpr::Eq(a,b), Op::Ne => RuleExpr::Ne(a,b),
        _ => RuleExpr::Compare(op,a,b),
    }
}
pub(super) fn admission(table: &Table) -> RuleExpr {
    let mut facts = vec![table.rules.read.clone()];
    for (column,op) in table.not_before.iter().map(|f|(f,Op::Lte)).chain(table.expire.iter().map(|f|(f,Op::Gt))) {
        let field=RuleTerm::Field(column.clone());
        let edge=comparison(op,field.clone(),clock());
        let edge=if table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(ColumnType::is_optional) {
            RuleExpr::Or(vec![RuleExpr::Eq(field,RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null)),edge])
        } else {edge};
        facts.push(edge);
    }
    RuleExpr::And(facts)
}

pub(super) fn bind(catalog: &Catalog, info: &super::super::catalog::TableInfo, expr: &ast::Expr) -> Option<RuleExpr> {
    use ast::{Expr,Literal,TypeKind};
    fn term(info:&super::super::catalog::TableInfo,e:&Expr)->Option<RuleTerm>{match e {
        Expr::Name{name,..} if name=="now" => Some(clock()),
        Expr::Member{base,name,..} if matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Focal{..}) && info.columns.get(name).is_some_and(|t|t.kind==TypeKind::Time) => Some(RuleTerm::Field(name.clone())),
        Expr::Literal{value:Literal::Number(n),..}=>Some(RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(n.parse().ok()?))),
        Expr::Literal{value:Literal::Null,..}=>Some(RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null)),
        _=>None,
    }}
    if let Expr::Binary{op,left,right,..}=expr {
        if op=="and"||op=="or" {
            let xs=vec![bind(catalog,info,left)?,bind(catalog,info,right)?];
            return Some(if op=="and"{RuleExpr::And(xs)}else{RuleExpr::Or(xs)});
        }
        let op=match op.as_str(){"="=>Op::Eq,"!="=>Op::Ne,"<"=>Op::Lt,"<="=>Op::Lte,">"=>Op::Gt,">="=>Op::Gte,_=>return None};
        if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(term(info,left),term(info,right)){return Some(comparison(op,a,b));}
    }
    if let Expr::Unary{op,value,..}=expr && op=="not" {return Some(RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(bind(catalog,info,value)?)));}
    let rule=ast::RuleDecl{effect:ast::Effect::Insert,bindings:Vec::new(),value:expr.clone(),site:expr.site().clone()};
    super::super::schema::bind_rule(catalog,info,&rule).ok()
}
//! Real upload ledgers for foreign-id and non-image placement challenges.
// @ref LLP 3000.038#3-upload — assets retain upload ownership and verified media type.
use super::*;
const PDF:&[u8]=b"%PDF-1.4\n%%EOF\n";
pub(super) fn file(e:&mut Engine,owner:&str,types:&[String])->Result<Value,String>{
    let mime=types.first().map(String::as_str).unwrap_or("application/pdf");
    let (bytes,name)=match mime{"application/pdf"=>(PDF,"proof.pdf"),"text/plain"=>(b"verified text\n".as_slice(),"proof.txt"),"image/png"=>(PNG.as_slice(),"proof.png"),_=>return Err(format!("file ledger needs a verified {mime} fixture"))};
    e.store_file(owner,bytes,Some(mime),name,NOW).map(|a|a.column_value()).map_err(|e|e.to_string())
}
fn contains(value:&Value,id:&Value)->bool {
    if value==id{return true;}
    match value{Value::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|v|contains(v,id)),Value::Map(m)=>m.values().any(|v|contains(v,id)),Value::Row(m)=>m.values().any(|v|contains(v,id)),_=>false}
}
pub(super) fn run(bound:&Bound,fixture:&Engine,out:&mut Campaign){
    let Ok(initial)=readback(fixture)else{return;};
    if initial.values().any(|xs|!xs.is_empty()){
        out.unsupported.insert("foreign-asset replay over populated fixtures needs full ledger transfer; empty-ledger challenges run separately".into());
    }
    for bp in bound.programs.iter().filter(|p|p.program.kind==ProgramKind::Mutation&&!p.program.native){
        let p=&bp.program;
        for (arg,kind) in &p.args{
            if !matches!(kind,ir::ArgType::Image{..}|ir::ArgType::File{..}){continue;}
            for mode in ["foreign upload","non-image photo"]{
                if mode=="non-image photo"&&!matches!(kind,ir::ArgType::Image{..}){continue;}
                let mut e=match engine(bound,&BTreeMap::new(),program("$security.asset",ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])){Ok(e)=>e,Err(error)=>{out.unsupported.insert(error);continue;}};
                let sample=if mode=="non-image photo"{file(&mut e,VIEWER,&[])}else{match kind{
                    ir::ArgType::Image{..}=>e.store_asset(OTHER,&PNG,NOW).map(|a|a.column_value()).map_err(|e|e.to_string()),
                    ir::ArgType::File{types,..}=>file(&mut e,OTHER,types),_=>unreachable!(),
                }};
                let sample=match sample{Ok(v)=>v,Err(error)=>{out.unsupported.insert(error);continue;}};
                let Some(mut args)=arguments(&p.args,0)else{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: asset challenge needs remaining argument fixtures",p.name));continue;};
                args.insert(arg.clone(),sample.clone());
                out.attempted+=1;*out.operations.entry(p.name.clone()).or_default()+=1;
                match e.mutate(&p.name,None,Context::new(VIEWER,args).at(NOW)){
                    Ok(result) if matches!(result.state,snapback4_core::WriteState::Failed{..})=>out.refused+=1,
                    Ok(_)=>{if let Ok(rows)=readback(&e)&&rows.values().flatten().any(|row|contains(row,&sample)){
                        out.boundary_failures.push(format!("{} placed {mode} through argument {arg}",p.name));
                    }},
                    Err(error)=>{out.unsupported.insert(format!("{}: asset challenge engine error {error}",p.name));},
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

pub trait Probe {
    fn reference(&mut self, _table: &str, _id: &Value, _field: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, crate::error::RuntimeError> { Ok(None) }
    /// Exact count-predicate transitions, distinct from ordinary witness expiry.
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { None }
    /// Exchange the private expiry accumulator; ordinary probes have none.
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, _expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { None }
    /// Coverage of the most recent leaf, consumed once by the shared evaluator.
    fn take_unknown(&mut self) -> bool { false }
    /// Called only when evaluation loads a field, after short-circuit decisions.
    fn field(&mut self, _field: &str, _old: bool) {}
    /// Apply the schema's point-key null rule before storage access. Timeline
    /// evaluation can visit operands that logical short-circuiting would skip.
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError>;
    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool;
}

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub enum Verdict { Readable, Denied, #[default] Unknown }

impl Verdict {
    fn not(self) -> Self {
        match self { Self::Readable => Self::Denied, Self::Denied => Self::Readable, Self::Unknown => Self::Unknown }
    }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, conjunction: bool) -> Self {
        let decisive = if conjunction { Self::Denied } else { Self::Readable };
        if self == decisive || other == decisive { decisive }
        else if self == Self::Unknown || other == Self::Unknown { Self::Unknown }
        else { decisive.not() }
    }
}

/// Evaluate a rule over a row image (and its old image, for update rules).
pub fn evaluate(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    evaluate_verdict(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe).map(|verdict| verdict == Verdict::Readable)
}

pub(super) fn evaluate_verdict(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Verdict, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let prior = probe.replace_expiry(None);
    let result = timeline(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe);
    probe.replace_expiry(match &result {
        Ok(answer) if answer.value == Verdict::Readable => earliest(prior, answer.changes.first().map(|(time, _)| *time)),
        _ => prior,
    });
    result.map(|answer| answer.value)
}

pub(super) fn earliest(a: Option<i64>, b: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> {
    match (a, b) { (Some(a), Some(b)) => Some(a.min(b)), (a, b) => a.or(b) }
}

struct Timeline {
    value: Verdict,
    changes: Vec<(i64, Verdict)>,
    // Proven from rule structure and verdicts, never from witness deadlines.
    invariant: bool,
}
impl Timeline {
    fn constant(value: Verdict) -> Self { Self { value, changes: Vec::new(), invariant: true } }
    fn combine(self, other: Self, conjunction: bool) -> Self {
        let (mut left, mut right) = (self.value, other.value);
        let mut result = Self::constant(left.combine(right, conjunction));
        let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
        result.invariant = (self.invariant && left == decisive)
            || (other.invariant && right == decisive) || (self.invariant && other.invariant);
        let (mut a, mut b) = (self.changes.as_slice(), other.changes.as_slice());
        let mut previous = result.value;
        while let Some(time) = earliest(a.first().map(|(t, _)| *t), b.first().map(|(t, _)| *t)) {
            // All rows expiring at the same instant disappear together. This
            // preserves correlations, including A or not A and A and not A.
            if let Some((head, tail)) = a.split_first() && head.0 == time { left = head.1; a = tail; }
            if let Some((head, tail)) = b.split_first() && head.0 == time { right = head.1; b = tail; }
            let value = left.combine(right, conjunction);
            if value != previous { result.changes.push((time, value)); previous = value; }
        }
        result
    }
}

// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#11 — observe the existing evaluation, never re-probe for diagnostics.
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub(super) fn evaluate_update(rule: &RuleExpr, both: bool, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<(bool, &'static str), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let prior = probe.replace_expiry(None);
    let result = if both {
        let RuleExpr::And(terms) = rule else { unreachable!("old_and_next is a conjunction") };
        compound_timeline(terms, true, viewer, old, next, false, probe)
            .map(|(answer, failed)| (answer, if failed == Some(0) { "old image" } else { "next image" }))
    } else {
        timeline(rule, viewer, old, next, false, probe).map(|answer| (answer, "named transition or identical old/next images"))
    };
    probe.replace_expiry(match &result {
        Ok((answer, _)) if answer.value == Verdict::Readable => earliest(prior, answer.changes.first().map(|(time, _)| *time)),
        _ => prior,
    });
    result.map(|(answer, image)| (answer.value == Verdict::Readable, image))
}

fn compound_timeline(terms: &[RuleExpr], conjunction: bool, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<(Timeline, Option<usize>), crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    let decisive = if conjunction { Verdict::Denied } else { Verdict::Readable };
    let mut answer = Timeline::constant(decisive.not());
    let mut failed = None;
    for (index, term) in terms.iter().enumerate() {
        let part = timeline(term, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?;
        if failed.is_none() && part.value != Verdict::Readable { failed = Some(index); }
        answer = answer.combine(part, conjunction);
        // Preserve the original timeline traversal and its physical allowance.
        if answer.value == decisive && answer.invariant { break; }
    }
    Ok((answer, failed))
}

// @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — next_expiry is the first expiry boundary that changes
// the complete bound rule's verdict, not a branch or first witness lifetime.
// Leaves prove their timeline within the original shared allowance. Combining
// those timelines performs no further storage reads.
fn timeline(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe) -> Result<Timeline, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::And(terms) | RuleExpr::Or(terms) => {
            let conjunction = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::And(_));
            compound_timeline(terms, conjunction, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe).map(|(answer, _)| answer)
        }
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => {
            let mut answer = timeline(inner, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe)?;
            answer.value = answer.value.not();
            for (_, value) in &mut answer.changes { *value = value.not(); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
        _ => {
            probe.take_changes();
            let mut missing = false;
            let value = evaluate_leaf(rule, viewer, old, next, created_allowed, probe, &mut missing)?;
            let expiry = probe.replace_expiry(None);
            let mut answer = Timeline::constant(if probe.take_unknown() || missing { Verdict::Unknown } else if value { Verdict::Readable } else { Verdict::Denied });
            if let Some(changes) = probe.take_changes() {
                answer.changes = changes;
                answer.invariant = false;
                return Ok(answer);
            }
            // An absent witness stays absent as rows expire. Positive probes
            // (including created facts with extra tests) need their alternatives
            // in both lifetime worlds; the observed deadline cannot decide this.
            answer.invariant = rule_probes(rule) == 0 || answer.value == Verdict::Denied;
            if answer.value == Verdict::Readable && let Some(time) = expiry { answer.changes.push((time, Verdict::Denied)); }
            Ok(answer)
        }
    }
}

fn evaluate_leaf(rule: &RuleExpr, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, created_allowed: bool, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<bool, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Public { .. } | RuleExpr::Allow => Ok(true),
        RuleExpr::Deny => Ok(false),
        RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? == term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) => Ok(term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)? != term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?),
        RuleExpr::Compare(op,left,right) => {
            let left = term(left, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let right = term(right, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?;
            let (Value::Int(a),Value::Int(b)) = (left,right) else { return Ok(false); };
            Ok(match op { crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte => a >= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gt => a > b,
                crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte => a <= b, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lt => a < b, _ => false })
        }
        RuleExpr::And(_) | RuleExpr::Or(_) | RuleExpr::Not(_) => unreachable!("compound rule"),
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
            // Bind each operand once, through one codec/allocation path for both leaves.
            let created = matches!(rule, RuleExpr::Created { .. });
            if created && !created_allowed {
                return Err(crate::error::RuntimeError::Schema("created probes are valid only in insert rules".into()));
            }
            let key = key.iter().map(|term| self::term(term, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            if created {
                if !probe.created(table, index, &key) { return Ok(false); }
                if equalities.is_empty() { return Ok(true); }
            }
            let equalities = equalities.iter().map(|(column, value)| Ok((column.clone(), term(value, viewer, old, next, probe, missing)?))).collect::<Result<Vec<_>, crate::error::RuntimeError>>()?;
            if *missing { return Ok(false); }
            probe.exists(table, index, &key, &equalities)

// One cached probe answer avoids refetching an unchanged fact in a loop.
// Writes invalidate it; cache hits still pay the declared logical probe charge.
#[derive(PartialEq)]
pub(super) struct WitnessKey {
    revision: u64, table: String, index: String, key: Vec<Value>,
    equalities: Vec<(String, Value)>, current: bool,
}

pub(super) struct RuleProbe<'a, 'b> {
    pub(super) runner: &'a mut Runner<'b>,
    pub(super) allowance: &'a Authorization,
}
impl Probe for RuleProbe<'_, '_> {
    fn reference(&mut self, table: &str, id: &Value, field: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> { self.runner.rule_reference(table, id, field) }
    fn take_changes(&mut self) -> Option<Vec<(i64, super::rules::Verdict)>> { self.runner.witness_changes.take() }
    fn replace_expiry(&mut self, expiry: Option<i64>) -> Option<i64> { self.runner.next_expiry.replace(expiry) }
    fn exists(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value], equalities: &[(String, Value)]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.runner.rule_probe_exists(self.allowance, table, index, key, equalities)
    }
    fn created(&mut self, table: &str, index: &str, key: &[Value]) -> bool {
        self.runner.created(table, index, key)
    }
}

/// The scanned prefix as the row a rule is judged against: each pinned column with its value.
fn prefix_row(index: &Index, prefix: &[Value]) -> Value {
    Value::Row(index.components.iter().zip(prefix).filter_map(|(part, value)| part.column().map(|name| (name.clone(), value.clone()))).collect::<std::collections::BTreeMap<_, _>>().into())
}

impl<'r> Runner<'r> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — the dependency envelope is schema-derived too; short-circuit probes cannot disclose a hidden answer through tables.
    pub(super) fn rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        for (table, _) in sites {
            if let Some(key) = self.schema.maintain_for(&table).and_then(|m|m.row_key.as_ref()) {
                self.read_tables.insert(key.table.clone());
            }
            self.aggregate_source_dependency(&table);
            self.read_tables.insert(table);
        }
    }

    fn metered_aggregate_source_dependency(&mut self, table: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if let Some(maintain) = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .filter(|maintain| self.schema.tables[&maintain.source].is_expiring()) {
            metered_dependency_table(&mut self.meter, &mut self.read_tables, &maintain.source)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    fn metered_rule_dependencies(&mut self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let site_heap = rule_probe_sites_heap(rule);
        let site_mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(site_heap)?;
//! The same fixture loader serves query testing and residency at either CLI entry point.
use super::Options;
use crate::project::Project;
use snapback4_core::{Engine, wire};
use snapback4_lang::Bound;

pub(super) fn engine(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    engine_at(project, bound, options, snapback4_core::now_ms())
}

pub(super) fn engine_at(project: &Project, bound: &Bound, options: &Options, now: i64) -> Result<Engine, String> {
    let Some(fixture) = options.flags.get("fixture") else {
        if options.flags.contains_key("source") {
            let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
            return Engine::new_at(store, bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|e| e.to_string());
        }
        return project.open_engine(bound).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open the store: {error}"));
    };
    let started = std::time::Instant::now();
    let mut schema = bound.schema.clone();
    // Residency defers ordinary maintains until after bulk loading and builds
    // them once; column derives stay installed and maintain as rows insert.
    let residency = options.flags.contains_key("residency");
    if residency { schema.maintains.retain(|m| m.private.is_some()); }
    let store = snapback4_core::Store::memory().map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut engine = Engine::new_at(store, schema, bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot open a store: {error}"))?;
    let mut names = Vec::new();
    for entry in std::fs::read_dir(fixture).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))? {
        let entry = entry.map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture directory {fixture}: {error}"))?;
        names.push(entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string());
    }
    names.sort();
    // One batch for the whole directory: the engine orders parents before
    // children within a batch, so file order (and read_dir order) cannot make
    // a read-through child or a reference miss its parent.
    let mut rows = Vec::new();
    let mut unknown = Vec::new();
    for name in names {
        let Some(table) = name.strip_suffix(".jsonl") else { continue };
        if name == super::test_cases::FILE { continue; }
        let path = std::path::Path::new(fixture).join(&name);
        let Some(definition) = bound.schema.tables.get(table) else {
            if options.flags.contains_key("saved-fixture-fallback") {
                eprintln!("test: skipped {} — saved source declares no table {table}; pass --fixture <dir> to select contemporary fixtures explicitly", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            // @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#7.4 — the starter's own unmodified sample is skipped loudly; a project's file for a missing table still refuses.
            if !options.flags.contains_key("source") && super::init::starter_fixture(&name).is_some_and(|starter| std::fs::read_to_string(&path).is_ok_and(|text| text == starter)) {
                eprintln!("test: WARNING: skipped {} — the todo starter's unmodified sample rows for a `{table}` table your snapback/*.q does not declare; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's other leftovers", path.display());
                continue;
            }
            unknown.push(name);
            continue;
        };
        let text = std::fs::read_to_string(&path).map_err(|error| format!("cannot read fixture file {}: {error}", path.display()))?;
        for (line_number, line) in text.lines().enumerate().filter(|(_, line)| !line.trim().is_empty()) {
            let at = format!("{name}:{}", line_number + 1);
            let mut json: serde_json::Value = serde_json::from_str(line).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}"))?;
            // A residency fixture is often an exported store: engine-maintained
            // columns are dropped and rebuilt from the source rows. `test`
            // refuses them as an insert and `/seed` do (E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN).
            if residency && let Some(fields) = json.as_object_mut() { fields.retain(|field, _| !definition.maintained.contains_key(field)); }
            relative_times(definition, &mut json, now).map_err(|why| format!("{at}: {why}"))?;
            if let Some(why) = wire::bare_principal(definition, &json) { return Err(format!("{at}: {why}")); }
            let row = wire::row_from_json(definition, &json).map_err(|error| format!("{at}: {error}\n  hint: a fixture row is one JSON object per line with the table's fields and an \"id\"; see snapback4 guide testing"))?;
            rows.push((table.to_owned(), row));
        }
    }
    // A misspelled file name would otherwise load nothing and still pass.
    if !unknown.is_empty() {
        let tables: Vec<&str> = bound.schema.visible_tables().map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).collect();
        // 0.4.17's init wrote the todo starter's rows beside a project's own schema.
        let starter = unknown.iter().any(|name| name == "todos.jsonl")
            && std::fs::read_to_string(std::path::Path::new(fixture).join("todos.jsonl")).is_ok_and(|text| text.contains("\"id\":\"todo-milk\""));
        let starter = if starter { format!("\n  hint: todos.jsonl is the todo starter's sample that `snapback4 init` wrote; `snapback4 init --adapt` removes it and the starter's {} while they are unmodified (or delete them, and the starter's list/add/toggle/remove lines, yourself)", super::test_cases::FILE) } else { String::new() };
        return Err(format!("{fixture}: {} name{} no table in snapback/*.q; nothing was run{starter}\n  hint: a fixture file is <table>.jsonl for a declared table ({}), plus {}; see snapback4 guide testing",
            unknown.join(", "), if unknown.len() == 1 { "s" } else { "" }, tables.join(", "), super::test_cases::FILE));
    }
    // Fixture rows take `/seed`'s insert path (defaults, sequences, unique with the author's codes, caps, maintained views), not the trusted bulk import.
    let loaded = engine.seed_at(rows, now).map_err(|error| {
        let text = error.to_string();
        let hint = if text.contains("NOT_FOUND") { "\n  hint: every fixture file loads as one batch, parents first; NOT_FOUND means a referenced or read-through parent row is in no fixture file (check the id)" } else { "" };
        format!("{fixture}: {text}{hint}")
    })?;
    if residency {
        engine.adopt_at(bound.schema.clone(), bound.programs.iter().map(|p| p.program.clone()), now).map_err(|error| format!("cannot build fixture maintains: {error}"))?;
    }
    if !options.json { println!("test: loaded {loaded} rows from {fixture} in {:.1} s", started.elapsed().as_secs_f64()); }
    Ok(engine)
}

// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.000#5-fixtures-and-dev-evidence — relative time leaves, seeds and operations share one captured clock.
/// Only typed time leaves interpret `now`: text and JSON retain their literals.
pub(crate) fn relative_times(table: &snapback4_core::schema::Table, row: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(fields) = row.as_object_mut() {
        for (name, value) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = table.columns.get(name) { time_value(kind, value, now).map_err(|why| format!("{name}: {why}"))?; }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub(super) fn time_value(kind: &snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType, value: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    use snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType;
    match (kind, value) {
        (ColumnType::Optional(inner), value) if !value.is_null() => time_value(inner, value, now),
        (ColumnType::List { of, .. } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, .. }, serde_json::Value::Array(items)) => {
            for item in items { time_value(of, item, now)?; }
            Ok(())
        }
        (ColumnType::Time, value) => {
            let Some(text) = value.as_str().filter(|text| text.starts_with("now")) else { return Ok(()) };
            let delta = if text == "now" { 0 } else {
                let offset = text.strip_prefix("now").unwrap();
                if !offset.starts_with(['+', '-']) { return Err(format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?}: expected now or now±duration (ms, s, m, h, d, w)")); }
                crate::dev_clock::offset(offset).map_err(|why| format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?}: {}", why.message))?
            };
            let resolved = now.checked_add(delta).filter(|time| (0..=snapback4_core::value::I53_MAX).contains(time))
                .ok_or_else(|| format!("E_FIXTURE_TIME: {text:?} is outside Unix milliseconds 0..9007199254740991"))?;
            *value = resolved.into();
            Ok(())
        }
        _ => Ok(()),
    }
}
pub(super) fn argument_times(kinds: &std::collections::BTreeMap<String, snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>, args: &mut serde_json::Value, now: i64) -> Result<(), String> {
    if let Some(fields) = args.as_object_mut() {
        for (name, value) in fields {
            if let Some(kind) = kinds.get(name).and_then(snapback4_core::schema::arg_as_column) {
                time_value(&kind, value, now).map_err(|why| format!("{name}: {why}"))?;
            }
        }
    }
    Ok(())
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod column_tests {
    use super::*;
    use snapback4_core::{Context, Value};
    #[test]
    fn residency_fixture_import_rebuilds_poisoned_columns() {
        let source=include_str!("../../../snapback4-lang/tests/fixtures/maintained-columns.q");
        let bound=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty());
        let root=std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("f1a-fixture-{}",std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        {
            let files=[("posts",r#"{"id":"p","at":1,"likeCount":999,"selected":999,"total":999}"#),
                ("likes",r#"{"id":"l","postId":"p","active":true,"amount":7}"#)];
            for index in [0,1] {std::fs::write(root.join(format!("{}.jsonl",files[index].0)),files[index].1).unwrap();}
            let options=Options {positionals:vec![],json:true,flags:[("fixture".into(),root.to_str().unwrap().into()),("residency".into(),String::new())].into()};
            let project=Project {root:root.clone()};
            let mut engine=engine(&project,&bound,&options).unwrap();
            let row=engine.query("postRow",Context::new("alice",[("id".into(),Value::Id("p".into()))].into()).at(1000)).unwrap().data;
            assert_eq!(["likeCount","selected","total"].map(|field|row.field(field).unwrap().as_int().unwrap()),[1,1,7]);
            assert_eq!(engine.store().rows("posts").unwrap(),vec![row]);
            for m in &engine.schema().maintains {
                let rows=engine.store().rows(&m.target).unwrap();
                assert_eq!(rows.len(),1);
                assert_eq!(rows[0].field("value").unwrap(),&Value::Int(if m.private.as_ref().unwrap().column=="total" {7} else {1}));
            }
            for (table,_) in files {std::fs::remove_file(root.join(format!("{table}.jsonl"))).unwrap();}
        }
        std::fs::remove_dir(&root).unwrap();
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod order_tests {
    use super::*;
    // `items` sorts before `lists`: the child's file is read first, yet the
    // directory loads as one batch with parents ordered before children.
    #[test]
    fn read_through_child_file_before_parent_file_loads() {
        let source = "use personas alice, bob\n\ntable lists:\n  owner: principal\n  title: text 1..80\n  immutable owner\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  cap 50 by owner\n  sync to .owner\n\ntable items:\n  parent: lists\n  text: text 1..200\n  immutable parent\n  read through .parent\n  insert <- .parent.owner = viewer\n  cap 50 by parent\n";
        let bound = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
        assert!(bound.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", bound.diagnostics);
        let root = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("fixture-order-{}", std::process::id()));
        std::fs::create_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        std::fs::write(root.join("items.jsonl"), "{\"id\":\"00000000000000000000000C01\",\"parent\":\"00000000000000000000000B01\",\"text\":\"wash\"}\n").unwrap();
        std::fs::write(root.join("lists.jsonl"), "{\"id\":\"00000000000000000000000B01\",\"owner\":\"dev:alice\",\"title\":\"Home\"}\n").unwrap();
        let options = Options { positionals: vec![], json: true, flags: [("fixture".into(), root.to_str().unwrap().into())].into() };
        let project = Project { root: root.clone() };
        let engine = engine(&project, &bound, &options);
        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&root).unwrap();
        let engine = engine.unwrap();
        assert_eq!(engine.store().rows("items").unwrap().len(), 1);
        assert_eq!(engine.store().rows("lists").unwrap().len(), 1);
    }

codex
The fixture campaigns use isolated store copies and keep synthetic witnesses non-failing. The main questions left are whether every claimed counterexample uses a stable policy judgment, and whether delegation advice distinguishes provisioning authority from ordinary membership or row ownership.
exec
/bin/bash -lc "sed -n '930,990p' llp/3000.036.004-security-invariants-and-exposure.rfc.md; sed -n '1050,1125p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/mod.rs; sed -n '1,85p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema/reference.rs; sed -n '880,940p' snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/ir.rs; rg -n 'fn seed|fn delete|final_exists|FinalExists|Immutable|recorded' snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/engine/ingress.rs; rg -n 'quarry|precedence' snapback4/crates/snapback4-core/src/schema/*.rs | head -40" in /home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3

Fixed-role comparison retains any additional probe context as an opaque
predicate; a matching role alone cannot make a region-qualified read globally
unconditional. Lifetime advice excludes a retained branch that already implies
the declared policy, including explicit owner alternatives. Arbitrarily named
role qualifiers require unambiguous literal-probe evidence before a compound
principal/qualifier key can be treated as a global roster.

Synthetic enumeration deduplicates identical candidate seed rows and typed
operation arguments after dependency pruning and literal pinning. Unrelated
schema enum digits cannot cause repeated replay of the same input. This is
finite input exploration, with no exhaustive entropy/generated-id claim.

A different or negated current-role check does not discharge retained-grant
intent: only implication of the applicable independent policy suppresses that
advice. Live roster probes are not themselves described as retained grants.
Repeated risky branches within one table/effect produce one located advisory;
audit retains their individual audiences and writers.

An initial column pass visits every sampled actor within its shared budget,
retaining each actor's first disclosure instead of stopping after the first
viewer. Later unrelated prefixes cannot replace an already observed zero-step
exposure for another sampled viewer.

Unprojected compiler-typed Row/Page payloads also witness column reads when the
returned field equals that committed physical value and its field-visibility
gate admits this viewer. This covers real media ids without manufacturing an
alternate asset ledger. Projected scalar/shape values retain the paired-world
requirement; a coincidentally matching constant is not column provenance. A
private field's redacted null is never treated as disclosure of a null value.

Absent contrary independent intent, caller-chosen role advice requires new
unconditional table authority; an additional trusted resource/ownership grant
is a scoped delegation boundary requiring its own reduction. Fixed enrollment
keeps the narrower read/delete/roster comparison. This avoids treating ordinary
self-enrollment for one's own records as privileged provisioning.

Principal-only global membership grants also participate in advice. An
unqualified viewer probe of a required unique principal denotes current
membership; authored deletion/principal reassignment can revoke it. Membership
creation receives escalation advice when it contradicts a pure caller-based
independent provisioning invariant (table insert/update/write or named operation).
Principal reassignment uses update authority including private-field ownership;
upsert creation and reassignment are judged on their separate arms. A dominating
global appointer role may reside in a separate roster, with all its qualifiers
retained when checking implication.
Ordinary account enrollment without such provisioning intent is not guessed to
be an administrator grant. Scoped/qualified membership still needs its reduction.

Paired query observations tag successful data separately from authored logical
refusals, retaining refusal codes and messages. Only engine-provided authored
rule/constraint markers qualify; incomplete reads, resource exhaustion and
ambiguous engine errors remain outside logical comparison. This prevents both
missed refusal oracles and collisions between a payload shape and a refusal.
Synthetic principal sampling separates the focal row's attribution from the
supporting roster/assignment recipient; finite enumeration still never proves
that all tuples are covered.
                return Err(error);
            }
        };
        if let Some(op) = op {
            let seq = match &outcome.state {
                WriteState::Sent { seq, .. } => *seq,
                WriteState::Failed { .. } => tx.next_seq()? - 1,
            };
            tx.record_applied(&viewer, op, seq, &serde_json::to_string(&outcome.receipt(Some(op))).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?)?;
            tx.bind_applied(&viewer, op, name, &wire_args, now)?;
        }
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(outcome)
    }

    /// Write rows as the system principal: rules are bypassed, validation,
    /// constraints, aggregates and partition events are not. One commit.
    pub fn seed(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.seed_at(rows, crate::now_ms())
    }

    /// Development hosts supply their clock so seed commits share operation and stream time.
    pub fn seed_at(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>, now: i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        let now = self.advance_time_audience(now)?;
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let now = time_audience::pin_writer_time(&self.schema, &tx, now)?;
        let count = seed_rows(&self.schema, &tx, rows, now)?;
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(count)
    }

    /// Delete a row as the system principal: constraints, aggregates and
    /// partition events apply. Returns the number of rows removed (0 or 1).
    pub fn seed_delete(&mut self, table: &str, id: &str) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.seed_delete_at(table,id,crate::now_ms())
    }
    /// Trusted deletion with the fixture/host clock, including publication and expiry.
    pub fn seed_delete_at(&mut self, table: &str, id: &str, now:i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_ephemeral_import(table)?;
        timeline::check_import(&self.schema, table)?;
        let now = self.advance_time_audience(now)?;
        let tx = self.store.transaction()?;
        let now = time_audience::pin_writer_time(&self.schema, &tx, now)?;
        let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &self.schema, Context::system().at(now), Meter::new(if crate::schema::live::has_live(&self.schema) {Ceilings::job()}else{Ceilings::unbounded()}, Cut::Abort), seq);
        if crate::schema::live::has_live(&self.schema) {runner.reserve_bulk_source(table)?;}
        let removed = match runner.delete(table, id) {
            Ok(()) => 1,
            Err(RuntimeError::Require { .. }) => 0,
            Err(error) => return Err(error),
        };
        let events = runner.events.take();
        let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
        drop(runner);
        if removed > 0 {
            tx.record_commit(seq, now, "seed", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "seed", &tables, removed)?;
            partition::append(&tx, seq, &events)?;
        }
        tx.commit()?;
        Ok(removed)
    }

    /// Untrusted fixture import. All targets are installed before attribution
    /// is captured, so import order cannot invent dangling references.
    pub fn load(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.load_mode(rows, LoadMode::Import)
    }

    /// Restore trusted durable images, including timeline and attribution history.
    pub fn load_trusted(&mut self, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        self.load_mode(rows, LoadMode::Restore)
    }

    /// Persistence restores keep historical attribution verbatim; imports
    /// cannot supply it. Device stores restore/page-apply at their own trusted
    /// transaction boundary with the same stored row representation.
use super::*;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum RuleTerm {
    /// One authorized point read through a row-local typed reference.
    Reference { table: String, column: String, field: String, old: bool },
    /// Bounded live reads; a distinct variant makes old decoders refuse.
    ReferenceChain { column: String, hops: Vec<RuleHop>, old: bool },
    Field(String),
    OldField(String),
    NextField(String),
    Viewer,
    Literal(Value),
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct RuleHop { pub table: String, pub field: String }

pub const MAX_RULE_HOPS: usize = 3;

impl RuleTerm {
    pub fn reference_source(&self) -> Option<(&str, bool)> {
        match self {
            Self::Reference { column, old, .. } | Self::ReferenceChain { column, old, .. } => Some((column, *old)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    pub fn references(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&str, &str)> {
        let (one, many) = match self {
            Self::Reference { table, field, .. } => (Some((table.as_str(), field.as_str())), &[][..]),
            Self::ReferenceChain { hops, .. } => (None, hops.as_slice()),
            _ => (None, &[][..]),
        };
        one.into_iter().chain(many.iter().map(|hop| (hop.table.as_str(), hop.field.as_str())))
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    pub fn has_reference(&self) -> bool {
        self.any_term(&|t| t.reference_source().is_some())
    }
    pub fn any_term(&self, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> bool) -> bool {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(term),
            Self::And(rs) | Self::Or(rs) => rs.iter().any(|r| r.any_term(term)),
            Self::Not(r) => r.any_term(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

pub(super) fn validate(schema: &Schema, source: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    let Some((column, old)) = term.reference_source() else { return; };
    let depth = term.references().count();
    let mut valid = !path.ends_with(".read") && (!old || update) && (1..=MAX_RULE_HOPS).contains(&depth)
        && !matches!(term, RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } if depth < 2);
    let (mut owner, mut column) = (source, column);
    for (table, field) in term.references() {
        valid &= schema.maintain_for(table).is_none()
            && matches!(owner.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: t }) if t == table)
            && !owner.maintained.contains_key(column) && !owner.written_by.get(column).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { valid = false; break; };
        valid &= target.ephemeral.is_none() && target.columns.contains_key(field)
            && !target.maintained.contains_key(field) && !target.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|a| !a.audience);
        owner = target; column = field;
    }
    if !valid { issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.into(), message: "E_QUARRY_RULE_HOP: write rules allow up to three ordinary live references with audience-readable fields; use authorized mutation reads for other paths".into() }); }
}

pub(super) fn validate_term(schema: &Schema, table: &Table, term: &RuleTerm, update: bool, path: &str, issues: &mut Vec<SchemaIssue>) {
    match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) => {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => {
            if !update {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: "old and next images exist only in update rules".into() });
            } else if !table.columns.contains_key(field) {
                issues.push(SchemaIssue { path: path.to_owned(), message: format!("rule reads undeclared column {field}") });
            }
        }
        RuleTerm::Reference { .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { .. } => validate(schema, table, term, update, path, issues),
        RuleTerm::Viewer | RuleTerm::Literal(_) => {}

/// Every table a program writes, by statement kind.
pub fn write_tables(program: &Program) -> Vec<(String, &'static str)> {
    let mut out = Vec::new();
    stmts_write_tables(&program.body, &mut out);
    out
}

   157	fn changed(bound:&Bound,p:&Program,before:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,after:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,initial:&BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>,viewer:&str,steps:&[Step],prior:&mut Option<Engine>,current:&mut Engine,out:&mut Campaign){

     4	pub(super) fn probe_pairs(bound:&Bound,table:&str,index:&str,key:&[RuleTerm],equalities:&[(String,RuleTerm)])->Vec<(String,RuleTerm)>{
     5	    let mut pairs=equalities.to_vec();
     6	    if let Some(idx)=bound.schema.table(table).and_then(|t|t.index(index)){for (component,term) in idx.components.iter().zip(key){if let Some(column)=component.column(){pairs.push((column.into(),term.clone()));}}}

     9	pub(super) fn global_principal(bound:&Bound,table:&str)->Option<String>{
    10	    let t=bound.schema.table(table)?;
    11	    t.indexes.values().find_map(|index|{
    12	        if !index.unique||index.components.len()!=1{return None;}
    13	        let column=index.components[0].column()?;
    14	        matches!(t.columns.get(column),Some(snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).then(||column.to_string())
    15	    })
    16	}
    17	pub(super) fn membership_probe(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{
    18	    let RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..}=rule else{return false;};
    19	    if table!=roster{return false;}
    20	    let Some(principal)=global_principal(bound,table)else{return false;};
    21	    let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
    22	    pairs.len()==1&&pairs[0].0==principal&&pairs[0].1==RuleTerm::Viewer
    23	}
    24	pub(super) fn membership_grant_risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,operation:&str,requirements:&[RuleExpr],op:ast::WriteOp)->bool{
    25	    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Insert)||membership_grant_risk(bound,roster,operation,requirements,ast::WriteOp::Update);}
    26	    fn honors(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str)->bool{match rule{
    27	        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>xs.iter().any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster)),
    28	        r=>membership_probe(bound,r,roster),
    29	    }}
    30	    if global_principal(bound,roster).is_none()||!bound.schema.tables.values().any(|t|[&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|honors(bound,r,roster))){return false;}
    31	    let table=&bound.schema.tables[roster];
    32	    let mut branches=match op {ast::WriteOp::Insert=>table.rules.insert.disjuncts(),ast::WriteOp::Update=>table.rules.update.disjuncts(),_=>return false}.into_iter().filter(|r|r.service_grant().is_none()).cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    33	    if op==ast::WriteOp::Update&&let Some(owner)=global_principal(bound,roster).and_then(|c|table.written_by.get(&c)){branches.push(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(owner.owner.clone()),RuleTerm::Viewer));}
    34	    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(RuleExpr::Or(branches)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    35	    let ordinary=substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster","$membership",None,None);
    36	    bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.is_none()&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).any(|p|super::operation::safe(p)&&kernel::implies(&ordinary,p)==Ok(false))
    37	}
    38	fn substitute(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str,column:&str,role:Option<&str>,target:Option<&str>)->RuleExpr{
    39	    let known=|t:&RuleTerm|match t{RuleTerm::Field(c) if c==column=>target.map(|s|snapback4_core::Value::String(s.into())),RuleTerm::Literal(v)=>Some(v.clone()),_=>None};
    40	    match rule{
    41	        RuleExpr::Exists{table,key,index,equalities,..} if table==roster&&key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)|t)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))=>{
    42	            let pairs=probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities);
    43	            let requested=pairs.iter().find(|(c,_)|c==column).and_then(|(_,t)|if let RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(s))=t{Some(s.as_str())}else{None});
    44	            if requested.is_none(){rule.clone()}else if requested==role{
    45	                if pairs.iter().all(|(c,t)|c==column||matches!(t,RuleTerm::Viewer)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{rule.clone()}
    46	            }else{RuleExpr::Deny}
    47	        },
    48	        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Id(seat)),RuleTerm::Viewer) if seat.starts_with("service:")=>RuleExpr::Deny,
    49	        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b)|RuleExpr::Ne(a,b)=>if let (Some(a),Some(b))=(known(a),known(b)){if (a==b)==matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Eq(..)){RuleExpr::Allow}else{RuleExpr::Deny}}else{rule.clone()},
    50	        RuleExpr::And(xs)=>RuleExpr::And(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
    51	        RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>RuleExpr::Or(xs.iter().map(|r|substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target)).collect()),
    52	        RuleExpr::Not(r)=>RuleExpr::Not(Box::new(substitute(bound,r,roster,column,role,target))),
    53	        _=>rule.clone(),
    54	    }
    55	}
    56	pub(super) fn risk(bound:&Bound,roster:&str,column:&str,operation:&str,targets:&[String],op:ast::WriteOp,requirements:&[RuleExpr],dynamic:bool)->Option<String>{
    57	    if op==ast::WriteOp::Upsert{return risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Insert,requirements,dynamic).or_else(||risk(bound,roster,column,operation,targets,ast::WriteOp::Update,requirements,dynamic));}
    58	    let table=bound.schema.table(roster)?;
    59	    if requirements.iter().any(|r|!r.disjuncts().is_empty()&&r.disjuncts().iter().all(|b|b.service_grant().is_some())){return None;}
    60	    let gates=match op{ast::WriteOp::Insert=>vec![&table.rules.insert],ast::WriteOp::Update=>vec![&table.rules.update],ast::WriteOp::Upsert=>vec![&table.rules.insert,&table.rules.update],ast::WriteOp::Delete=>return None};
    61	    let roles=match table.columns.get(column)?.required(){snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Enum{variants}=>variants.clone(),snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Text{..}=>{
    62	        let mut roles=BTreeSet::new();
    63	        fn collect(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,roster:&str,column:&str,roles:&mut BTreeSet<String>){match rule{
    64	            RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if table==roster=>for (c,t) in &probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities){if c==column&&let RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(s))=t{roles.insert(s.clone());}},
    65	            RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles)},RuleExpr::Not(r)=>collect(bound,r,roster,column,roles),_=>{}
    66	        }}
    67	        for t in bound.schema.tables.values(){for r in [&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete]{collect(bound,r,roster,column,&mut roles);}}roles.into_iter().collect()
    68	    },_=>return None};
    69	    let intents=bound.invariants.iter().filter(|i|i.table==roster&&i.column.as_deref().is_none_or(|c|c==column)&&(i.operation.as_deref()==Some(operation)||i.operation.is_none()&&(i.target=="write"||i.target==if op==ast::WriteOp::Insert{"insert"}else{"update"}))).filter_map(|i|i.policy.as_ref()).collect::<Vec<_>>();
    70	    fn role_guards(bound:&Bound,rule:&RuleExpr,out:&mut Vec<RuleExpr>){match rule{
    71	        RuleExpr::Exists{table,index,key,equalities,..} if key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,v)|v)).any(|k|matches!(k,RuleTerm::Viewer))&&probe_pairs(bound,table,index,key,equalities).iter().any(|(c,v)|role_columns(bound,table).contains(c)&&matches!(v,RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::String(_))))=>out.push(rule.clone()),
    72	        RuleExpr::And(xs)|RuleExpr::Or(xs)=>for r in xs{role_guards(bound,r,out)},_=>{}
    73	    }}
    74	    let mut all_branches=gates.iter().flat_map(|r|r.disjuncts()).filter(|b|b.service_grant().is_none()).cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    75	    if op!=ast::WriteOp::Insert&&let Some(owner)=table.written_by.get(column){all_branches.push(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(owner.owner.clone()),RuleTerm::Viewer));}
    76	    let admission=RuleExpr::And(std::iter::once(RuleExpr::Or(all_branches)).chain(requirements.iter().cloned()).collect());
    77	    let mut guards=Vec::new();role_guards(bound,&admission,&mut guards);
    78	    let independent=kernel::implies(&substitute(bound,&admission,"$no-roster",column,None,None),&RuleExpr::Or(guards))==Ok(true);
    79	    for target in roles.iter().filter(|r|targets.is_empty()||targets.contains(r)){
    80	        for caller in std::iter::once(None).chain(roles.iter().map(|s|Some(s.as_str()))){
    81	            if caller==Some(target.as_str()){continue;}
    82	            let admitted=substitute(bound,&admission,roster,column,caller,Some(target));
