//! Expanded helpers can write from any expression block.
// @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — legacy eligibility examines the complete expanded write footprint.
use super::*;
pub(super) fn stmts_write_tables(stmts: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    for stmt in stmts {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Insert { table, .. } | Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "insert")),
            Stmt::Update { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "update")),
            Stmt::Upsert { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "upsert")),
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, upsert, .. } => out.push((target.table.clone(), if *upsert { "upsert" } else { "update" })),
            Stmt::Delete { table, .. } => out.push((table.clone(), "delete")),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => out.push((target.table.clone(), "delete")),
            _ => {}
        }
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Const { value, .. } | Stmt::Let { value, .. } | Stmt::Assign { value, .. } | Stmt::Push { value, .. } | Stmt::Return(value) => expr_write_tables(value, out),
            Stmt::Emit { payload: value, once, .. } => { expr_write_tables(value, out); if let Some(value) = once { expr_write_tables(value, out); } },
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                stmts_write_tables(then_body, out);
                stmts_write_tables(else_body, out);
            }
            Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => {
                match domain {
                    LoopDomain::Page(scan) => scan_write_tables(scan, out),
                    LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => { expr_write_tables(start, out); expr_write_tables(end_exclusive, out); }
                    LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, .. } => expr_write_tables(value, out),
                    LoopDomain::Recurse(recurse) => {
                        expr_write_tables(&recurse.seed, out);
                    }
                    LoopDomain::LiteralRange { .. } | LoopDomain::ArgList { .. } => {}
                }
                stmts_write_tables(body, out);
            }
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expr_write_tables(condition, out),
            Stmt::Check { condition, current } => {
                expr_write_tables(condition, out);
                for key in &current.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::InsertIfAbsent { row, .. } | Stmt::Insert { row, .. } => expr_write_tables(row, out),
            Stmt::Update { key, patch, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
            }
            Stmt::Upsert { key, row, .. } => {
                expr_write_tables(key, out);
                expr_write_tables(row, out);
            }
            Stmt::WriteTarget { target, patch, condition, .. } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
                expr_write_tables(patch, out);
                if let Some(condition) = condition { stmts_write_tables(&condition.setup, out); expr_write_tables(&condition.value, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Delete { key, .. } => expr_write_tables(key, out),
            Stmt::DeleteTarget { target } => {
                for key in &target.key { expr_write_tables(key, out); }
            }
            Stmt::Break | Stmt::Continue => {}
        }
    }
}

fn scan_write_tables(scan: &Scan, out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    if let Some(filter) = &scan.filter { expr_write_tables(filter, out); }
    if let Some(range) = &scan.range {
        for bound in [&range.gt, &range.gte, &range.lt, &range.lte].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(bound, out); }
    }
    for key in &scan.prefix {
        match key { IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) | IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(expr) => expr_write_tables(expr, out) }
    }
    for cursor in [&scan.after, &scan.before].into_iter().flatten() { expr_write_tables(cursor, out); }
}

fn expr_write_tables(expr: &Expr, out: &mut Vec<(String, &'static str)>) {
    match expr {
        Expr::Literal(_) | Expr::Variable(_) | Expr::Input(_) => {}
        Expr::Field { base, .. } => expr_write_tables(base, out),
        Expr::Array(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)),
        Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)),
        Expr::Shape { base, fields } => { expr_write_tables(base, out); fields.values().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)); }
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => {
            expr_write_tables(left, out);
            expr_write_tables(right, out);
        }
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } => expr_write_tables(value, out),
        Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out)),
        Expr::Get(read) | Expr::Exists(read) => {
            read.key.iter().for_each(|item| expr_write_tables(item, out));
        }
        Expr::Block { body, value } => { stmts_write_tables(body, out); expr_write_tables(value, out); }
        Expr::Scan(scan) => scan_write_tables(scan, out),
        Expr::Merge(merge) => {
            if let Some(after) = merge.position.as_ref().and_then(|position| position.after.as_ref()) { expr_write_tables(after, out); }
            for lane in &merge.lanes {
                if let Some(guard) = &lane.guard { expr_write_tables(guard, out); }
                if let Some(project) = &lane.project { expr_write_tables(project, out); }
                if let Some(value) = &lane.distinct { expr_write_tables(value, out); }
                stmts_write_tables(&lane.setup, out);
                if let Some(source) = &lane.source {
                    if let Some(scan) = source.scan() { scan_write_tables(scan, out); }
                    for value in source.expressions() { expr_write_tables(value, out); }
                }
                scan_write_tables(&lane.scan, out);
            }
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn seed_rows(schema: &Schema, tx: &crate::store::Tx<'_>, rows: impl IntoIterator<Item = (String, Value)>, now: i64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
    let bounded=crate::schema::live::has_live(schema);
    let mut bulk_meter=Meter::new(if bounded {Ceilings::job()}else{Ceilings::unbounded()},Cut::Abort);
    let rows:Box<dyn Iterator<Item=(String,Value)> + '_>=if bounded {Box::new(live_bulk::collect(rows,&mut bulk_meter)?.into_iter())}else{Box::new(rows.into_iter())};
    let rows = through_activation::ancestor_order(schema, rows);
    let targets: std::collections::BTreeSet<_> = schema.tables.values().flat_map(|table| table.recorded.values().map(|field| field.table.as_str())).collect();
    let mut imports = BTreeMap::<(String, String), Value>::new();
    let rows: Box<dyn Iterator<Item = (String, Value)> + '_> = if targets.is_empty() { Box::new(rows.into_iter()) } else {
        let rows: Vec<_> = rows.into_iter().collect();
        // A capture sees the imported target's eventual image, including
        // fields retained by patches, defaults and assigned ordinals.
        // Preview every allocation in input order (counters cross tables),
        // then undo the preview; ordinary writes allocate in that same
        // transaction and still own all validation, events and rollback.
        tx.savepoint("seed_sequences")?;
        let mut staging = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), bulk_meter, 0);
        for (name, row) in &rows {
            if bounded {staging.reserve_bulk_source(name)?;staging.meter.heap(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(&schema.tables[name]).saturating_mul(4))?;}
            schema.refuse_ephemeral_import(name)?;
            let table = schema.table(name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {name}")))?;
            sequences::refuse_fields(table, name, row)?;
            let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
            let old = match imports.get(&(name.clone(), id.clone())) { Some(row) => Some(row.clone()), None => tx.get(name, &id)? }.filter(|row| !table.expired(row, now));
            let mut fields = old.as_ref().map(|row| row.as_object().cloned()).transpose()?.unwrap_or_default();
            fields.extend(row.as_object()?.clone());
            if old.is_none() { for (field, default) in &table.defaults { fields.entry(field.clone()).or_insert_with(|| default.value(now, crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL)); } }
            let mut image = Value::Row(fields.into());
            if old.is_none() {
                let (assigned, full) = staging.sequence_fields(name, image.import_input(&ImportInput(())))?;
                if full { return Err(sequences::exhausted()); }
                staging.commit_sequences(name, &assigned)?;
                image = assigned;
            }
            imports.insert((name.clone(), id), image);
        }
        bulk_meter=staging.meter.clone();
        drop(staging);
        tx.rollback_to("seed_sequences")?;
        Box::new(rows.into_iter())
    };
    let seq = tx.next_seq()?;
    let mut runner = Runner::new(tx, schema, Context::system().at(now), bulk_meter, seq);
    runner.import_targets = Some(&imports);
    let mut count = 0;
    for (table, row) in rows {
        if bounded {runner.reserve_bulk_source(&table)?;}
        schema.refuse_ephemeral_import(&table)?;
        timeline::check_import(schema, &table)?;
        runner.upsert(&table, &row.field("id")?.clone(), row.import_input(&ImportInput(())))?;
        count += 1;
    }
    // Seeds preserve ordinary expired images for a later sweep. A source of
    // a row-keyed view is the established exception: an already-expired
    // seeded image must not enter a committed count basis or revive after
    // a clock rollback. Route only those sources through the common retirement
    // path so reference actions and net maintenance still run transactionally.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#6.5 — seeded expired row-view sources retire with actions; unrelated expired fixtures remain stored.
    let final_exists = runner.satisfied_final_exists()?;
    runner.retire_seeded_view_sources()?;
    // Seeds historically admit fixture rows whose obligations are not
    // meaningful under the system viewer. Retirement may not invalidate an
    // obligation that did hold immediately before its reference actions.
    runner.check_final_exists_witnesses(&final_exists)?;
    let events = runner.events.take();
    let tables: Vec<String> = runner.written_tables.iter().cloned().collect();
    drop(runner);
    if count > 0 {
        tx.record_commit(seq, now, "seed", crate::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL, "seed", &tables, count)?;
        partition::append(tx, seq, &events)?;
    }
    Ok(count)
    }

// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — durable and ephemeral seats receive the same static clause for every private cause.
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub fn seat_refusal(schema:&Schema,error:&RuntimeError,viewer:&str)->Refusal {
    let mut why=error.refusal();
    if viewer.starts_with("service:") && let RuntimeError::Rule {table,effect}|RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget {table,effect}=error {
        if let Some(def)=schema.table(table) {why.message.push_str(&format!("; {table} rule clause: {effect} <- {}",def.rules.for_effect(*effect).quarry()));}
    }
    why
}

mod update_image_tests;
//! Writes: the four words, their rules, the constraints (unique, cap,
//! immutable, append-only, after-insert), maintained aggregates, and the
//! partition events every row change produces.

use super::runner::Runner;
use super::validate::validate_row;
use crate::error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError};
use crate::partition;
use crate::rows::index_values;
use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir};
use crate::schema::{Constraint, Effect, Table, materialize_optional_fields};
use crate::value::{Supplied, Value, canonical_json};

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(crate) enum Origin {
    Source,
    Maintained,
    LiveAuthority,
}

#[derive(Clone)]
pub(super) struct FinalExistsWitness {
    table: String,
    row: Value,
    constraint: usize,
}

#[path = "write_references.rs"]
mod references;
#[path = "write_target.rs"]
pub(crate) mod target;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn insert(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, false).map(|_| ())
    }

    pub(super) fn insert_if_absent(&mut self, table: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.insert_mode(table, row, true)
    }

    fn insert_mode(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        if self.device && if_absent && self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("insert if absent on a read-through child queues without a row".into()));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            let mark = runner.meter.mark();
            let result = runner.insert_attempt(table_name, row, if_absent, None).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, Effect::Insert));
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — denied direct allocations hide deferred
            // read witnesses and counter work just as NOT_FOUND hides targets.
            if matches!(&result, Err(RuntimeError::Rule { effect: Effect::Insert, .. } | RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. }))
                && runner.schema.tables.get(table_name).is_some_and(|table| !table.sequences.is_empty()) {
                runner.meter.restore(mark);
            }
            result
        })
    }

    fn insert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: Supplied, if_absent: bool, upsert_index: Option<&str>) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Insert)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, true)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        let supplied_sequence = super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row);
        // A directly supplied field is a payload error before any lookup;
        // only an upsert's copied address waits for insert authority.
        if upsert_index.is_none() && supplied_sequence.is_err() { return supplied_sequence.map(|_| false); }
        let supplied = row.clone();
        let row = self.with_defaults(table_name, row)?;
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].ephemeral.is_some() { self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes())?; }
        let row = row.ephemeral(&self.schema.tables[table_name], self.context.now.unwrap_or(0))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.014#4-the-capture — reserve, then stamp the parent capture before any rule sees the image.
        self.reserve_capture(&self.schema.tables[table_name])?;
        let row = self.capture_insert(table_name, row)?;
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        let independent = !table.sequences.is_empty() && !super::rules::deciding_fields(table_name, table, &table.rules.insert)
            .iter().any(|field| table.sequences.contains_key(field));
        // An independent denial must never consult a hidden read witness.
        let authorized = independent && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)?;
        if independent && !authorized {
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
        }
        if !table.sequences.is_empty() {
            // Admission reserves the worst case before either read verdict;
            // restoring a refusal alone cannot hide a boundary abort.
            // An independent insert rule was already prepaid and evaluated.
            // Only still-pending checks belong in this preflight allowance.
            let insert_checks = if authorized { 0 } else { 1 + u64::from(upsert_index.is_some() && supplied_sequence.is_ok()) };
            self.reserve_sequence_authorization(table, &row, insert_checks)?;
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — an unreadable allocation cannot expose counter,
        // uniqueness or cap state, even to a principal allowed to insert.
        if !self.sequence_insert_readable(table_name, &row)? {
            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "sequence read authority");
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "sequence read authority") });
        }
        if let Err(error) = supplied_sequence {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — absent upserts decide insert authority before field diagnostics.
            // A target key may supply an ordinal even when the patch does not.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
                return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule") });
            }
            return Err(if upsert_index.is_some() { self.no_write_target() } else { error });
        }
        if upsert_index.is_some() {
            self.authorize_fields(table_name, &row, &supplied).map_err(|_| self.no_write_target())?;
        } else { self.authorize_fields(table_name, &row, &supplied)?; }
        // The image copy an event slot costs is the writer's own supplied bytes (LLP 3000.015 §1).
        let image_bytes = row.heap_bytes();
        let (row, sequence_full) = self.sequence_fields(table_name, row)?;
        let row = self.receipt_insert_fields(table_name, row)?;
        if let Some(index) = upsert_index {
            if self.device { self.prove_upsert_insert(table_name, index, &row)?; }
            // Preserve upsert's target preflight before the ordinary insert checks;
            // only this first authority refusal is an undisclosed target miss.
            if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? { return Err(self.no_write_target()); }
            if sequence_full { return Err(super::sequences::exhausted()); }
            // Engine event slots are stamped after predecessor/sequence checks,
            // just as on an ordinary insert; preflight validates authored fields.
            if let Err(error) = super::validate::validate_before_slots(self.schema, table_name, &row) {
                return Err(if matches!(error, RuntimeError::Input(_)) && !self.context.is_system() { self.no_write_target() } else { error });
            }
        }
        if !authorized && !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Insert, None, &row)? {
            return Err(self.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "insert rule"));
        }
        // Retry keys independent of engine allocation still identify a no-op
        // at the ceiling. Never use the clamped authority candidate as a key.
        if sequence_full && if_absent {
            let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
            for (name, index) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(_, index)| {
                index.unique && index.columns().iter().all(|column| !table.sequences.contains_key(column))
            }) {
                let key = index_values(index, &row);
                if key.contains(&Value::Null) { continue; }
                if self.collision_lookup(table_name, name, index, &key, None, Effect::Insert)?.is_some() {
                    self.observe_sequence_columns(table_name, &row, &index.columns(), true)?;
                    return Ok(false);
                }
            }
        }
        if sequence_full { return Err(super::sequences::exhausted()); }
        let row = self.record_insert(table_name, row)?;
        let row = self.with_asset_facts(table_name, row, None, None)?;
        if if_absent {
            for (name, index) in table.indexes.iter().filter(|(_, index)| index.unique) {
                let key = index_values(index, &row);
                if key.iter().any(|value| *value == Value::Null) {
                    continue;
                }
                if self.collision_lookup(table_name, name, index, &key, None, Effect::Insert)?.is_some() {
                    self.observe_sequence_columns(table_name, &row, &index.columns(), true)?;
                    return Ok(false);
                }
            }
        }
        let id = row.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();

            table.indexes.iter().find(|(name, index)| {
                    name.as_str() != "byId" && index.unique && !index_values(index, &row).contains(&Value::Null)
                })
                .map(|(name, _)| name.as_str()).or_else(|| {
                    table.constraints.iter().any(|c| matches!(c, Constraint::Cap { .. })).then_some("byId")
                })
        } else { Some("byId") };

            self.prove_upsert_insert(table_name, index, &row)?;

        // and reserved both target proofs. Retiring an expired predecessor
        // uses that same allowance, not a third read-rule reservation.
        let collision_probes = if upsert_index.is_some() { 0 } else { super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read) };
        let has_through_children = self.schema.children(table_name).next().is_some();
        let has_children = has_through_children || self.schema.is_referenced(table_name);
        let predecessor = self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, collision_probes)], |runner, allowance| {

                // reference retirement. A declared read-through child is
                // different: a non-reader cannot probe whether hidden
                // physical children retain the predecessor.
                else if has_through_children && !runner.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, row)? {
                    return Err(runner.rule_refusal(table_name, Effect::Insert, "unique id"));
                }
            }

    pub(super) fn update(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            runner.update_attempt(table_name, key, patch)
        })
    }

    fn update_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, patch: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Update)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &patch, false)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &patch)?;
        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &patch)?;
        if patch.as_object()?.contains_key("id") {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} update patch cannot change id")));
        }
        let id = key.as_str()?;
        self.reserve_target_resolution(table_name, self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?, &patch, false)?;
        let Some(old) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| {
            runner.fetch_write_target(allowance, table_name, id)
        })? else {
            return Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()]));
        };
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].sync.is_none() { return Err(self.write_miss(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()])); }
        let readable = !self.inactive_write_target(table_name, &old)?
            && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
        let patch = self.ignore_hidden_immutable_patch(table_name, patch, readable)?;
        let (new, copy) = self.merge_patch(table_name, &old, &patch, readable)?;
        self.replace(table_name, old, new, readable, &patch, copy)
    }

    pub(super) fn upsert(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| {
            runner.upsert_attempt(table_name, key, row)
        })
    }

    fn upsert_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, key: &Value, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_upsert_owned(table_name)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_fields(table_name, &row, false)?;
        self.refuse_recorded(table_name, &row)?;
        super::sequences::refuse_fields(&self.schema.tables[table_name], table_name, &row)?;
        let id = key.as_str()?;
        if row.field("id")?.as_str()? != id {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Input(format!("{table_name} upsert row id must equal its key")));
        }
        self.reserve_target_resolution(table_name, self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?, &row, true)?;
        match self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| {
            runner.fetch_write_target(allowance, table_name, id)
        })? {
            Some(old) => {
                let readable = !self.inactive_write_target(table_name, &old)?
                    && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
                let row = self.ignore_hidden_immutable_patch(table_name, row, readable)?;
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — an existing row receives a patch; omitted columns keep their values.
                let (new, copy) = self.merge_patch(table_name, &old, &row, readable)?;
                self.replace(table_name, old, new, readable, &row, copy)
            }
            None if self.device && !self.upsert_absence_proven(table_name, "byId", &[key.clone()])? =>
            {
        super::validate::validate_projection(self.schema, table_name, &new, self.device.then_some(self.context.viewer.as_str()))?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).expect("validated");
        let entries = self.maintain_scratch(self.contribution_heap(table), |runner| {
            runner.tx.put(table_name, table, &new, Some(&old))
        })?;
        self.charge_source_indexes(table, Some(&old), Some(&new), entries)?;
        self.row_changed(table_name, Some(&old), Some(&new), Origin::Source)
    }

    pub(super) fn delete(&mut self, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.device && self.schema.tables[table_name].not_before.is_some() {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(
                "a mutation touching a `not before` table runs on the server".into(),
            ));
        }
        self.target_attempt(table_name, |runner| runner.delete_attempt(table_name, id))
    }

    fn delete_attempt(&mut self, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_engine_owned(table_name, Effect::Delete)?;
        self.refuse_receipt_scope_delete(table_name)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        self.reserve_write_rules(table, &[Effect::Read, Effect::Delete])?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.9 — delete prepays hidden contribution work before fetching its old image, with the same attempt lifetime as update.
        self.reserve_maintains(table_name, table)?;
        let Some(old) = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| {
            runner.fetch_write_target(allowance, table_name, id)
        })? else {
            return Err(RuntimeError::Require { site: Some(self.statement_site.clone()), code: "NOT_FOUND".to_owned(), message: None });
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — a delete that consults referencing rows needs read authority on its row first.
        let readable =
            !self.inactive_write_target(table_name, &old)? && self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &old)?;
        if (self.schema.children(table_name).next().is_some() || self.schema.is_referenced(table_name)) && !readable
        {
            self.refusing_clause(table_name, "read rule");
            return Err(self.no_write_target());
        }
        if !self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Delete, None, &old)? {
            return Err(if readable {
                RuntimeError::Rule { table: table_name.to_owned(), effect: Effect::Delete }
            } else { self.no_write_target() });
        }
        self.delete_authorized(table_name, old)
    }

    pub(super) fn delete_authorized(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let old = if self.schema.is_referenced(table_name) {
            self.apply_reference_actions(table_name, &old)?;
            // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — the actions may have changed the root (a maintained column mirrored from its children): remove its current image.
            let id = old.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned();
            self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, &id))?.unwrap_or(old)
        } else { old };
        self.delete_row(table_name, old)
    }

    /// A delete's own removal of one row, its rules already decided: receipt
    /// membership, the read-through child probe, constraints, maintains and
    /// the partition event. A referential cascade removes each row this way.
    pub(super) fn delete_row(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
    /// A disjunct with `viewer = .col` spelled `.col = viewer`, for folding repeats.
    pub fn normal_disjunct(&self) -> RuleExpr {
        match self {
            Self::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, other) => Self::Eq(other.clone(), RuleTerm::Viewer),
            other => other.clone(),
        }
    }

    /// The rule as Quarry text, for diagnostics and rewrites.
    pub fn quarry(&self) -> String {
        fn term(term: &RuleTerm) -> String {
            match term {
                RuleTerm::Reference { table, column, field, old } => format!("{table}[{}.{column}].{field}", if *old { "old" } else { "" }),
                RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column, hops, old } => hops.iter().fold(format!("{}.{column}", if *old { "old" } else { "" }), |key, hop| format!("{}[{key}].{}", hop.table, hop.field)),
                RuleTerm::Field(f) => format!(".{f}"),
                RuleTerm::OldField(f) => format!("old.{f}"),
                RuleTerm::NextField(f) => format!("next.{f}"),
                RuleTerm::Viewer => "viewer".to_owned(),
                RuleTerm::Literal(Value::String(s)) => format!("'{s}'"),
                RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Id(s)) => format!("'{s}'"),
                RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Bool(b)) => b.to_string(), RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Int(n)) => n.to_string(), RuleTerm::Literal(Value::Null) => "null".to_owned(), // Quarry, never `{"Bool":true}`
                RuleTerm::Literal(v) => crate::value::canonical_json(v),
            }
        }
        match self {
            Self::Public { .. } => "public".to_owned(),
            Self::Allow => "allow".to_owned(),
            Self::Deny => "deny".to_owned(),
            Self::Eq(l, r) => format!("{} = {}", term(l), term(r)),
            Self::Compare(op,l,r) => format!("{} {} {}", term(l), match op { crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte => ">=", crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gt => ">", crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte => "<=", _ => "<" }, term(r)),
            Self::Ne(l, r) => format!("{} != {}", term(l), term(r)),
            Self::And(items) => items.iter().map(|i| if matches!(i,Self::Or(_)){format!("({})",i.quarry())}else{i.quarry()}).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and "),
            Self::Or(items) => items.iter().map(|i| i.quarry()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or "),
            Self::Not(inner) => format!("not ({})", inner.quarry()),
            Self::Exists { table, key, equalities, .. } => format!("exists {table}[{}]", key.iter().map(term).chain(equalities.iter().map(|(col, value)| format!("{col} = {}", term(value)))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
            Self::Created { table, key, equalities, .. } => format!("created {table}[{}]", key.iter().map(term).chain(equalities.iter().map(|(col, value)| format!("{col} = {}", term(value)))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
        }
    }
}

pub use reference::{RuleTerm, RuleHop, MAX_RULE_HOPS};

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub enum Constraint {
    /// Shared decoder fence. Only the schema codec writes this wire carrier.
    Capabilities(BTreeSet<String>),
    /// Decode-only marker from unpublished F2 stores; the schema codec removes it.
    #[serde(skip_serializing)]
    Recorded,
    Unique { index: String },
    Cap { by: Vec<String>, max: u32 },
    AppendOnly,
    Immutable { fields: Vec<String> },
    Text { field: String, #[serde(default)] min: u32, #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")] format: Option<TextFormat> },
    /// After every statement and before commit, the indexed target point
    /// must exist for each row this transaction inserted (`after insert`).
//! Field authority and projection share one schema fact on every host.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — the owner is a principal on this row, not a row update grant.
use super::*;

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct FieldAuthority {
    pub owner: String,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "std::ops::Not::not")]
    pub audience: bool,
}

impl Table {
    pub fn private_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        self.written_by.get(field).is_some_and(|authority| !authority.audience)
    }

    pub fn field_owners(&self, row: &Value) -> BTreeSet<String> {
        self.written_by.values().filter(|a| !a.audience).filter_map(|a|
            row.field(&a.owner).ok().and_then(|v| v.as_str().ok()).map(str::to_owned)).collect()
    }
}

// Private values cannot enter an index key (including cursors), an audience,
// attribution, or a materialized aggregate's ordinary readable output. This
// also checks resolved/inferred indexes and raw schemas, not just syntax.
pub fn validate(schema: &Schema) -> Vec<SchemaIssue> {
    let mut issues = Vec::new();
    let mut issue = |name: &str, field: &str, why: String| issues.push(SchemaIssue {
        path: format!("tables.{name}.columns.{field}"), message: format!("written by: {name}.{field} {why}") });
    for (name, table) in &schema.tables {
        for (field, authority) in &table.written_by {
            if !table.columns.contains_key(field) || field == "id" || table.columns.get(&authority.owner) != Some(&ColumnType::Principal)
                || table.written_by.contains_key(&authority.owner) || table.maintained.contains_key(field) || table.recorded.contains_key(field) {
                issue(name, field, format!("needs an ordinary required principal owner column .{} on the same table", authority.owner));
            }
            if !table.defaults.contains_key(field) {
                issue(name, field, "needs a declared default for inserts by other principals".into());
            }
            if authority.audience { continue; }
            if table.indexes.values().any(|index| index.uses_column(field)) || table.expire.as_ref() == Some(field) {
                issue(name, field, "is private and cannot supply an index/cursor or lifetime; declare readable by audience to use it there".into());
            }
            for timeline in &schema.timelines {
                let mut dependencies = BTreeSet::new();
                for mapping in [&timeline.authors[..], &timeline.posts[..], &timeline.reposts[..], &timeline.edges[..]] {
                    if mapping[0] == *name { dependencies.extend(mapping[1..].iter().cloned()); }
                }
                if timeline.posts[0] == *name && let Some(filter) = &timeline.filter { filter.fields(&mut dependencies); }
                if dependencies.contains(field) {
                    issue(name, field, "is private and cannot supply a timeline mapping or predicate; declare readable by audience first".into());
                }
            }
            for maintain in &schema.maintains {
                if let Some(key) = &maintain.row_key
                    && key.table == *name && key.fields().contains(field)
                    && !requires_owner(&table.rules.read, &authority.owner) {
                    issue(name, field, "is private and can feed a row-keyed count only when the member read rule requires its owner; restrict the member audience or declare readable by audience".into());
                }
                if maintain.source == *name && maintain.dependencies().contains(field) {
                    issue(name, field, "is private and cannot feed a readable aggregate; declare readable by audience first".into());
                }
            }
        }
        for (other_name, other) in &schema.tables {
            for capture in other.recorded.values() {
                if capture.table == *name && table.private_field(&capture.column) { issue(other_name, &capture.column, "cannot record another row's private field".into()); }
            }
        }
        for (effect, rule) in [("read", &table.rules.read), ("insert", &table.rules.insert), ("update", &table.rules.update), ("delete", &table.rules.delete)] {
            private_rule(schema, name, rule, effect == "update" && table.update_transition, &mut issue);
        }
    }
    issues
}

// Every admitted member reader must also be allowed to see the predicate input.
// A conjunctive owner grant proves that; an alternative grant does not.
fn requires_owner(rule: &RuleExpr, owner: &str) -> bool {
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(field), RuleTerm::Viewer)
        | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(field)) => field == owner,
        RuleExpr::And(parts) => parts.iter().any(|part| requires_owner(part, owner)),
        _ => false,
    }
}

fn private_rule(schema: &Schema, name: &str, rule: &RuleExpr, update: bool, issue: &mut impl FnMut(&str, &str, String)) {
    let term = |term: &RuleTerm, issue: &mut dyn FnMut(&str, &str, String)| {
        if !update && let RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::OldField(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) = term
            && schema.tables[name].private_field(field) {
            issue(name, field, "is private; only a named update (old, next) transition may use it as a rule input; declare readable by audience for other rules".into());
        }
    };
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => { term(a,issue); term(b,issue); }
        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => for item in items { private_rule(schema,name,item,update,issue); },
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => private_rule(schema,name,inner,update,issue),
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, key, equalities, .. } => {
            for item in key { term(item,issue); }
            for (field,value) in equalities {
                term(value,issue);
                if schema.table(table).is_some_and(|t|t.private_field(field)) { issue(table,field,"is private and cannot be probed from a rule".into()); }
            }
        }
        _ => {}
    }
}

impl RuleExpr {
    /// Comparing an untouched field with itself needs no knowledge of its
    /// value. Every other use (including a probe key) requires field visibility.
    pub fn needs_field_value(&self, field: &str, kind: &ColumnType) -> bool {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(name) | RuleTerm::OldField(name) | RuleTerm::NextField(name) if name == field);
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) => term(a) != term(b),
            // null is not ordered, even against itself. Its presence is private.
            Self::Compare(_,a,b) => (term(a) || term(b)) && (kind.is_optional() || term(a) != term(b)),
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|rule|rule.needs_field_value(field, kind)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.needs_field_value(field, kind),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,value)|value)).any(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }

    /// Fold only proven comparisons of an untouched, invisible field. This
    /// avoids interpreting an omitted device value as null or as its default.
    pub fn fold_untouched_field(&mut self, field: &str, kind: &ColumnType) {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(name) | RuleTerm::OldField(name) | RuleTerm::NextField(name) if name == field);
        let constant = match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) if term(a) && term(b) => Some(true),
            Self::Ne(a,b) if term(a) && term(b) => Some(false),
            Self::Compare(op,a,b) if !kind.is_optional() && term(a) && term(b) =>
                Some(matches!(op, crate::ir::BinaryOp::Gte | crate::ir::BinaryOp::Lte)),
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => {
                for item in items { item.fold_untouched_field(field,kind); }
                None
            }
            Self::Not(inner) => { inner.fold_untouched_field(field,kind); None }
            _ => None,
        };
        if let Some(value) = constant { *self = if value { Self::Allow } else { Self::Deny }; }
    }

    pub fn references_field(&self, field: &str) -> bool {
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| matches!(term, RuleTerm::Field(name) | RuleTerm::OldField(name) | RuleTerm::NextField(name) if name == field);
        match self {
            Self::Eq(a,b) | Self::Ne(a,b) | Self::Compare(_,a,b) => term(a) || term(b),
            Self::And(items) | Self::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|rule| rule.references_field(field)),
            Self::Not(inner) => inner.references_field(field),
            Self::Exists { key, equalities, .. } | Self::Created { key, equalities, .. } => key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,value)|value)).any(term),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
}

            }

            probe.exists(table, index, &key, &equalities)
        }

pub(super) fn term(term: &RuleTerm, viewer: &str, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value, probe: &mut dyn Probe, missing: &mut bool) -> Result<Value, crate::error::RuntimeError> {
    Ok(match term {
        RuleTerm::Field(field) | RuleTerm::NextField(field) => { probe.field(field, false); next.field(field).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::OldField(field) => { probe.field(field, true); old.and_then(|old| old.field(field).ok()).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null) },
        RuleTerm::Reference { column, old: previous, .. } | RuleTerm::ReferenceChain { column, old: previous, .. } => {
            probe.field(column, *previous);
            let row = if *previous { old } else { Some(next) };
            let mut value = row.and_then(|r| r.field(column).ok()).cloned().unwrap_or(Value::Null);
            for (table, field) in term.references() {
                match probe.reference(table, &value, field)? {
                    Some(next) => value = next,
                    None => { *missing = true; return Ok(Value::Null); }
                }
            }
            value
        },
        RuleTerm::Viewer => Value::Id(viewer.to_owned()),
        RuleTerm::Literal(value) => value.clone(),
    })
}

/// The schema-derived probe allowance, independent of short-circuit answers.
pub(crate) fn rule_probes(rule: &RuleExpr) -> u64 {
    let hops = |terms: &[&RuleTerm]| terms.iter().map(|t| t.references().count() as u64).sum::<u64>();
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => hops(&[a,b]),
        RuleExpr::Exists { key, equalities, .. } => 1 + key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)| t)).map(|t| hops(&[t])).sum::<u64>(),
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — created facts with extra equalities take the metered exists path when the fact holds.
        RuleExpr::Created { key, equalities, .. } => u64::from(!equalities.is_empty()) + key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)| t)).map(|t| hops(&[t])).sum::<u64>(),
        RuleExpr::And(terms) | RuleExpr::Or(terms) => terms.iter().map(rule_probes).fold(0, u64::saturating_add),
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => rule_probes(inner),
        RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Deny | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => 0,
    }
}

/// A rule's probe sites, as `(table, index)`: what a rule reads besides the
/// row, for live subscriptions and the partition audience.
pub fn probe_sites(rule: &RuleExpr, out: &mut Vec<(String, String)>) {
    fn term(t: &RuleTerm, out: &mut Vec<(String, String)>) {
        for (table, _) in t.references() { out.push((table.into(), "byId".into())); }
    }
    match rule {
        RuleExpr::Eq(a,b) | RuleExpr::Ne(a,b) | RuleExpr::Compare(_,a,b) => { term(a,out); term(b,out); }
        RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } | RuleExpr::Created { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
            out.push((table.clone(), index.clone()));
            for t in key.iter().chain(equalities.iter().map(|(_,t)| t)) { term(t,out); }
        }
        RuleExpr::And(items) | RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().for_each(|item| probe_sites(item, out)),
        RuleExpr::Not(inner) => probe_sites(inner, out),
        _ => {}
    }
}

    fn collision_lookup(&mut self, table: &str, name: &str, index: &crate::schema::Index, key: &[Value], except: Option<&str>, effect: Effect) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let probes = super::rules::rule_probes(&self.schema.tables[table].rules.read);
        self.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, probes)], |runner, allowance| {
            let found = if runner.schema.tables[table].not_before.is_some() {
                runner.lookup_write_occupant(table, name, index, key)?
            } else {
                runner.live_lookup(allowance, table, name, index, key, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
            };
            if let Some(row) = &found && Some(row.field("id")?.as_str()?) != except {
                runner.observe_sequence_columns(table, row, &index.columns(), effect == Effect::Insert)?;
                runner.visible_collision(table, row, effect, &format!("unique {}", index.columns().join(", ")))?;
            }
            Ok(found)
        })
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000#7-decisions-during-execution — hidden collisions use the ordinary write-rule refusal
    fn visible_collision(
        &mut self,
        table: &str,
        row: &Value,
        effect: Effect,
        clause: &str,
    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let mut image = row.clone();
        let definition = self.schema.table(table).expect("validated");
        materialize_optional_fields(&mut image, definition);
        if self.inactive_write_target(table, &image)? || !self.check_rule(table, Effect::Read, None, &image)? {
            return Err(self.rule_refusal(table, effect, clause));
        }
        Ok(())
    }

//! Author-owned intent starts with a reviewable draft, never inferred policy.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#103-adoption — syntactic coverage is distinct from a proved invariant.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn missing(bound:&Bound)->Vec<String>{
    bound.schema.visible_tables().filter(|(name,t)| {
        ([&t.rules.read,&t.rules.insert,&t.rules.update,&t.rules.delete].iter().any(|r|!matches!(r,RuleExpr::Deny)) || !t.written_by.is_empty())
            && !bound.invariants.iter().any(|i| &i.table==*name)
    }).map(|(name,_)|name.clone()).collect()
}

/// Suggested declarations mirror enforcement, not the product specification.
/// The caller must keep this outside the compiled .q source set.
pub fn draft(bound:&Bound)->String{
    let mut out=String::from("-- DRAFT: NOT ACCEPTED. Generated from access rules, not the app specification.\n-- Confirm or correct each policy against the spec, then copy chosen declarations\n-- into an authored snapback/*.q file. This .q.draft file is never compiled.\n-- Check current roles, grant revocation, count/existence privacy, deadlines and\n-- required evidence separately. A copied wrong rule cannot catch itself.\n-- snapback4 guide security\n\n");
    for (name,t) in bound.schema.visible_tables(){
        for (effect,rule) in [("read",&t.rules.read),("insert",&t.rules.insert),("update",&t.rules.update),("delete",&t.rules.delete)] {
            if matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Deny)||covered(bound,name,effect){continue;}
            let predicate=if matches!(rule,RuleExpr::Public{..}){"allow".into()}else{rule.quarry()};
            if predicate.contains('$') || effect=="update" && t.update_transition {
                out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}.{effect}: write intent explicitly; captured/transition rule: {predicate}\n"));
            }else {out.push_str(&format!("-- Candidate from {name} {effect} rule; AUTHOR MUST CONFIRM.\ninvariant {name}.{effect} only {predicate}\n"));}
        }
        for (column,authority) in &t.written_by {
            out.push_str(&format!("-- {name}.{column}: effective field-owner patch authority .{} = viewer bypasses the table update rule; declare column-write intent from the spec.\n",authority.owner));
        }
    }
    out
}
fn covered(bound:&Bound,table:&str,effect:&str)->bool{
    bound.invariants.iter().any(|i|i.table==table && i.operation.is_none() && i.column.is_none()
        && (i.target==effect || i.target=="write" && matches!(effect,"insert"|"update")))
}

    pub(super) fn insert_upsert(&mut self, table_name: &str, addressed_index: &str, row: Supplied) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.refuse_upsert_owned(table_name)?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#5 — trusted fixture imports preserve the constraint layer.
        if self.context.is_system() {
            return self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()).map_err(|error| match error {
                RuntimeError::Input(message) => RuntimeError::Constraint(message),
                other => other,
            });
        }
        if self.schema.tables[table_name].through.is_some() {
            // A read-through child's insert arm keeps the hidden-target word for every authority refusal.
            return self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()).map_err(|e| match e {
                RuntimeError::Rule {..} | RuntimeError::Input(_) => self.no_write_target(), other => other,
            });
        }
        match self.insert_attempt(table_name, row, false, Some(addressed_index)).map(|_| ()) {
            Err(RuntimeError::Input(_)) => Err(self.no_write_target()),
            result => result,
        }
    }

    fn replace(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: Value, new: Value, readable: bool, patch: &Value, copy: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.authorize_update_fields(table_name, &old, &new, patch, readable)?;
        self.replace_authorized(table_name, old, new, readable, patch, copy)
    }

    /// `copy` is the merged image's lawful price, charged again for an engine slot copy.

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — ruling 17 (a), third pass: hidden immutable fields are ignored, never compared, on update and upsert.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn ignore_hidden_immutable_patch(&self, table: &str, patch: crate::value::Supplied, readable: bool) -> Result<crate::value::Supplied, RuntimeError> {
        if readable { return Ok(patch); }
        patch.remove_fields(self.schema.tables[table].constraints.iter().filter_map(|c|match c {
            crate::schema::Constraint::Immutable {fields} => Some(fields.iter().cloned()), _ => None,
        }).flatten())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#14 — ruling 17 (b): an author condition needs a readable target; a blind patch needs none.
    fn require_readable_condition(&self, table: &str, readable: bool) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if readable { Ok(()) } else { self.refusing_clause(table, "read rule"); Err(self.no_write_target()) }
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#17 — the runtime also forbids writes while engine old is in scope.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn refuse_condition_write(&self) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        if self.evaluating_write_condition { Err(RuntimeError::Input("an update condition cannot write".into())) } else { Ok(()) }
    }

//! Closed enum census: a new IR statement/expression/builtin forces review.
// @ref LLP 3000.036.004#3-sound-proof-boundary — opaque context changes are blockers.
use snapback4_core::ir::{Expr,Stmt,Builtin};
pub(super) fn body(xs:&[Stmt])->bool{xs.iter().all(|s|match s{
    Stmt::Const{value,..}|Stmt::Let{value,..}|Stmt::Assign{value,..}|Stmt::Push{value,..}|Stmt::Return(value)=>expr(value),
    Stmt::If{condition,then_body,else_body}=>expr(condition)&&body(then_body)&&body(else_body),
    Stmt::For{..}=>false,
    Stmt::Require{condition,..}|Stmt::Check{condition,..}=>expr(condition),
    Stmt::Insert{row,..}|Stmt::InsertIfAbsent{row,..}|Stmt::Upsert{row,..}=>expr(row),
    Stmt::Update{key,patch,..}=>expr(key)&&expr(patch),
    Stmt::WriteTarget{patch,condition,..}=>expr(patch)&&condition.as_ref().is_none_or(|c|body(&c.setup)&&expr(&c.value)),
    Stmt::Delete{key,..}=>expr(key),Stmt::DeleteTarget{..}|Stmt::Break|Stmt::Continue=>true,
    Stmt::Emit{..}=>false,
})}
fn expr(e:&Expr)->bool{match e{
    Expr::Literal(_)|Expr::Variable(_)|Expr::Input(_)=>true,
    Expr::Field{base,..}|Expr::Unary{value:base,..}=>expr(base),
    Expr::Array(xs)=>xs.iter().all(expr),Expr::Object(xs)=>xs.values().all(expr),
    Expr::Shape{base,fields}=>expr(base)&&fields.values().all(expr),
    Expr::Binary{left,right,..}=>expr(left)&&expr(right),
    Expr::Block{body:xs,value}=>body(xs)&&expr(value),
    Expr::Get(r)|Expr::Exists(r)=>r.key.iter().all(expr),
    Expr::Scan(_)|Expr::Merge(_)=>false,
    Expr::Builtin{builtin,args}=>pure(builtin)&&args.iter().all(expr),
}}
fn pure(b:&Builtin)->bool{match b{
    Builtin::JobAs|Builtin::JobQuery{..}=>false,
    Builtin::ProjectFields{..}|Builtin::ReceiptSettled{..}|Builtin::OptionalField{..}|Builtin::Calendar{..}|Builtin::Civil(_)|Builtin::Len|Builtin::Slice|Builtin::Concat|Builtin::Lower|Builtin::Trim|Builtin::Sha256|Builtin::Join|Builtin::Group{..}|Builtin::First|Builtin::Last|Builtin::At|Builtin::Min|Builtin::Max|Builtin::Extremum{..}|Builtin::ExtremumOf{..}|Builtin::Divmod{..}|Builtin::Divide{..}|Builtin::Sort{..}|Builtin::MentionTokens|Builtin::SearchPresent|Builtin::RequireUncapped|Builtin::DeterministicShare|Builtin::Count|Builtin::Sum|Builtin::CountCapped|Builtin::Connected=>true,
}}

    pub(super) fn inactive(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.inactive_with_pending(table_name, row, true)
    }

    /// A physical write target may be cancelled or rescheduled while its
    /// publication is pending. It remains unreadable to the author's program.
    pub(super) fn inactive_write_target(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        self.inactive_with_pending(table_name, row, false)
    }

    /// Step over a fetched row that is expired at this run's clock but not yet
    /// swept. It is the store's internal work: it never reaches the public
    /// meter or the shared authorization walk, so a read meters the same on
    /// every store and on a device that never received the row. The work is
    /// bounded by its own guard: each stepped row reserves a fixed 256 bytes
    /// of internal scratch plus its key, so one execution steps over at most
    /// about a million such rows before it refuses E_STORE_MEMORY.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.6 — the public meter charges only rows live at the read's clock.
    /// Returns whether `row` was expired (and so stepped over).
    pub(super) fn step_inactive(&mut self, table_name: &str, row: &Value, key: &[u8]) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if !self.inactive(table_name, row)? { return Ok(false); }
        let bytes = EXPIRED_STEP + key.len() as u64;
        self.tx.refund(1);
        self.expired_steps = self.expired_steps.saturating_add(bytes);
        self.meter.scratch = self.meter.scratch.saturating_add(bytes);
        if self.expired_steps <= EXPIRED_STEP_GUARD { return Ok(true); }
        Err(word_heap::store_memory())
    }

    /// An unopened rule witness is absent without spending the shared
    /// authorization walk. Expired witnesses retain their established
    /// privacy bound; callers check the remaining upper lifetime separately.
            _ => Ok(None),
        }
    }

    /// A server write may address an unopened row without making that image
    /// readable. Fixed decode admission prevents its payload size from
    /// selecting a public meter result.
    pub(crate) fn fetch_write_target(
        &mut self,
        allowance: &Authorization,
        table_name: &str,
        id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        if self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .is_none_or(|table| table.not_before.is_none())
        {
            return self.fetch(allowance, table_name, id);
        }
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        if self.device {
            self.unknown_fact(
                table_name,
                "byId",
                &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())],
                "scheduled write target",
            );
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let found = self.authorized_scratch(
            crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table),
            &[],
            |runner, _| {
                runner.tx.get(allowance, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                    found.map(|mut row| {
                        materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
        }
        self.authorized_scratch(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table), &[], |runner, scope| {
                runner.live_lookup(scope, table_name, name, index, key, #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)
            },
        )
    }

    /// @ref LLP 3000.036.003#6 — write constraints keep lower-lifetime occupants physical.
    /// Write constraints and server write targets see an unopened row as a
    /// physical occupant. They still pass expired siblings because expiry,
    /// unlike a lower read lifetime, releases logical uniqueness.
    pub(in crate::engine) fn lookup_write_occupant(&mut self, table_name: &str, name: &str, index: &Index, key: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        if table.not_before.is_none() { return self.lookup_stored(table_name, name, index, key, false); }
        let reservation = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table);
        let mut bounds = Bounds::default();
        loop {
            let found = self.authorized_scratch(reservation, &[], |runner, scope| {
                runner.tx.scan(scope, table_name, name, key, &bounds, Dir::Asc, 1)
                    .map(|mut rows| rows.pop())
            })?;
            let Some((mut row, stored)) = found else { return Ok(None); };
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, &self.schema.tables[table_name]);
            if !self.expired(&self.schema.tables[table_name], &row) { return Ok(Some(row)); }
            bounds.after = crate::key::decode(&stored).map(|values| values.into_iter().skip(key.len()).collect());
        }
    }

    /// The scan's prefix values, evaluated. A word term is not a value a
    /// store can seek to; `scan_words` takes those scans apart first.
    pub(super) fn prefix_values(&mut self, scan: &Scan) -> Result<Vec<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        scan.prefix
            .iter()
            .map(|key| match key {
                IndexKeyExpr::Scalar(expr) => self.expr(expr),
                IndexKeyExpr::WordPrefix(_) => Err(RuntimeError::Schema("a matching term is read through its words(..) index, never as a prefix value".into())),
            })
            .collect()
    }

    /// A `matching` read: the query's first term selects every word of the
    /// index it is a prefix of (one seek each, bounded by the meter for a
//! Historical reference attribution. Capture belongs to insertion/activation;
//! every contribution evaluator continues to read the source row image.
// @ref LLP 3000.007.003#3.2 — captures survive target deletion and generation rebuilds.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, schema::Table, value::Value};

pub(crate) fn refuse_fields(table: &Table, name: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    if let Some(field) = row.as_object()?.keys().find(|field| crate::schema::timeline::is_slot(field)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_TIMELINE_OWNED", message: format!("{name}.{field} is an engine event slot; omit it and write the post or repost's application fields (guide feeds)") });
    }
    if let Some(field) = row.as_object()?.keys().find(|field| table.engine_field(field)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_RECORDED_FIELD", message: format!("{name}.{field} is engine-recorded; omit it from the write payload (guide maintains)") });
    }
    if let Some(field) = row.as_object()?.keys().find(|field| table.maintained.contains_key(*field)) {
        return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_MAINTAINED_COLUMN", message: format!("{name}.{field} is engine-maintained; omit it and write the source rows (guide maintains)") });
    }
    Ok(())
}

pub(crate) fn public_image(table: &Table, mut row: Value) -> Value {
    if table.ephemeral.is_none() && table.recorded.is_empty() && table.through.is_none() && !table.columns.keys().any(|f| crate::schema::timeline::is_slot(f)) { return row; }
    if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row { fields.retain(|field, _| {
            !table.engine_field(field) && !crate::schema::timeline::is_slot(field)
        }); }
    row
}

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn refuse_recorded(&self, table: &str, row: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let definition = self.schema.table(table).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table}")))?;
        refuse_fields(definition, table, row)
    }

    pub(crate) fn capture_recorded(&mut self, table: &Table, mut row: Value, paths: &[String]) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        if paths.is_empty() { return Ok(row); }
        row.as_object()?;
        // A source image can share its map with a literal or stored image;
        // stamping fields may copy it. Account for that copy and each read.
        self.meter.heap(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(table))?;
        let source_image = crate::schema::row_image(&row, table).into_owned();
        self.meter.heap(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(table))?;
        for path in paths {
            let field = &table.recorded[path];
            let target = &self.schema.tables[&field.table];
            // Even a null reference has the same declared point-read charge.
            self.meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
            self.read_tables.insert(field.table.clone());
            let found = match source_image.field(&field.reference) {
                Ok(Value::Id(id)) => {
                    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
                    let imported = self.import_targets.and_then(|rows| rows.get(&(field.table.clone(), id.clone()))).cloned();
                    #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
                    let imported: Option<Value> = None;
                    match imported { Some(row) => Some(row), None => self.lookup_stored(&field.table, "byId", target.index("byId").expect("id index"), &[Value::Id(id.clone())], false)? }
                }
                _ => None,
            };
            let live = match found.as_ref() {
                Some(value) if !self.inactive(&field.table, value)? => Some(value),
                _ => None,
            };
            if self.device && matches!(source_image.field(&field.reference), Ok(Value::Id(_)))
                && (target.sync.is_none() || !live.zip(self.context.now).map(|(value, now)| {
                    self.device_work(|runner| {
                                runner.authorized_scratch(0,&[],|runner,scope| {
                                    runner.tx.recording_covered(&field.table, value, &runner.context.viewer, now, scope, &mut runner.meter)
                                })
                            })
                }).transpose()?.unwrap_or(false)) {
                return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("recorded path {path} needs a live, covered {} row; reconnect to resolve attribution", field.table)));
            }
            // Unknown coverage queues before a target-size resource refusal.
            // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — a public capture cannot price a private sibling.
            if found.is_some() {
                self.meter.heap(super::write_target::row_copy_ceiling(target))?;
            }
            let value = live.map(|value| {
                    self.disclosed_row(target,value.clone()).into_value().field(&field.column).cloned()
                }).transpose()?.unwrap_or(Value::Null);
            self.meter.heap(80 + path.len() as u64 + super::write_target::column_copy_ceiling(&target.columns[&field.column]))?;
            if let Value::Row(fields) | Value::Map(fields) = &mut row { fields.insert(path.clone(), value); }
        }
        Ok(row)
    }

    pub(super) fn record_insert(&mut self, name: &str, row: Value) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
        if self.schema.tables[name].recorded.is_empty() { return Ok(row); }
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        let paths = table.recorded.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
        self.capture_recorded(table, row, &paths)
    }
}

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub(super) fn table_paths_changed(next: Option<&Table>, old: Option<&Table>) -> bool {
    next.into_iter().flat_map(|t| &t.recorded).ne(old.into_iter().flat_map(|t| &t.recorded))
}

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
pub(super) fn paths_changed(schema: &crate::schema::Schema, previous: Option<&crate::schema::Schema>) -> bool {
    schema.tables.keys().chain(previous.into_iter().flat_map(|s| s.tables.keys()))
        .any(|name| table_paths_changed(schema.table(name), previous.and_then(|s| s.table(name))))
}

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn rebuild_recorded_activation(&mut self, previous: Option<&crate::schema::Schema>, maintains: &[&crate::schema::Maintain], report: &mut crate::store::adoption_report::AdoptionReport) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let saved = self.event_free;
        self.event_free = true;
        let result = (|| {
            for maintain in maintains {
                if table_paths_changed(self.schema.table(&maintain.source), previous.and_then(|s| s.table(&maintain.source))) { self.rebuild_maintain_reported(maintain, report)?; }
            }
            Ok(())
        })();
        self.event_free = saved;
        result
    }

        use crate::rows::{Bounds, Dir};
        if self.device { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction("recorded activation requires the server's replacement snapshot".into())); }
        for (name, table) in &self.schema.tables.clone() {

   124	            (C::File{types,..},Value::String(marker)) if marker=="$security:file"=>assets::file(engine,VIEWER,types),
   125	            _=>Ok(value.clone()),
   126	        }
   127	    }
   128	    let mut seeded=rows.iter().filter(|(name,_)|bound.schema.maintain_for(name).is_none()).map(|(name,rs)|{
   129	        let t=&bound.schema.tables[name];
   130	        let clean=rs.iter().map(|r|if let Value::Row(fields)=r {Value::Row(fields.iter().filter(|(c,_)|!t.engine_field(c)&&!t.recorded.contains_key(*c)&&!t.maintained.contains_key(*c)).map(|(c,v)|(c.clone(),v.clone())).collect())}else{r.clone()}).collect();
   131	        (name.clone(),clean)
   132	    }).collect::<BTreeMap<String,Vec<Value>>>();

   134	        if let Ok(v)=row.field(column) {
   135	            let value=hydrate(&mut engine,kind,v)?;

   137	        }
   138	    }}}

   140	    let actual=readback(&engine)?;

   151	    let name="$security.probe";let table=inv.table.clone();let key=id(&table);
   152	    let read=Read{site:"security challenge".into(),table:table.clone(),index:"byId".into(),key:vec![Expr::Literal(key.clone())],provenance:KeyProvenance::Computed};
   153	    let mut seeds=rows.clone();
   154	    let probe=match word{
   155	        "read"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Query,vec![Stmt::Return(Expr::Get(read))]),
   156	        "insert"=>{seeds.remove(&table);program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Insert{table,row:Expr::Literal(row.clone())},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))])},
   157	        "delete"=>program(name,ProgramKind::Mutation,vec![Stmt::Delete{table,key:Expr::Literal(key)},Stmt::Return(Expr::Literal(Value::Null))]),

   636	fixture histories instead fork the complete SQLite store and asset ledger into
   637	owned isolated memory stores; captures, deadlines, maintained values, lifetimes
   638	and actual media identities survive unchanged. No source store is modified.
   639	Temporary asset directories belong to Store and disappear on drop.
   640	
   641	Actors include all principal values in fixtures. Required/optional references
   642	range over fixture rows, not only the first. Short histories visit first steps
   643	breadth first before extending them under a reported work cap. Read and row

   645	actual committed changes, correct insert/update/delete arms and pre-write

   647	need executed-effect tracing and remain unsupported. A finite trace is never

impl Drop for Store {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        if self.ephemeral_assets {
            let _ = std::fs::remove_dir_all(&self.assets_dir);
        }
    }
}

remaining disclosures are explicit uncovered work. Mutation sequence replay
continues within its own bounds. Read-call exhaustion never grants a proof.

Tuple-key role tests use the same column-aware normalization in lifetime advice.
A scoped enum context is not a role qualifier; qualifying unique keys contain
one principal and actual role qualifiers only. Named-operation delegation intent
is honored for that caller operation. Initial fixture column observations run
before history prefixes and synthetic exploration; continuation shares the same
work counters so a later phase cannot evict a zero-step witness by consuming its
budget first. §12.8's independent-current-role requirement supersedes the older
name-based alternative in §12.6.

Fixed-role comparison retains any additional probe context as an opaque
predicate; a matching role alone cannot make a region-qualified read globally
unconditional. Lifetime advice excludes a retained branch that already implies
the declared policy, including explicit owner alternatives. Arbitrarily named
role qualifiers require unambiguous literal-probe evidence before a compound
principal/qualifier key can be treated as a global roster.

Synthetic enumeration deduplicates identical candidate seed rows and typed
operation arguments after dependency pruning and literal pinning. Unrelated
schema enum digits cannot cause repeated replay of the same input. This is
finite input exploration, with no exhaustive entropy/generated-id claim.

A different or negated current-role check does not discharge retained-grant
intent: only implication of the applicable independent policy suppresses that
advice. Live roster probes are not themselves described as retained grants.
Repeated risky branches within one table/effect produce one located advisory;
audit retains their individual audiences and writers.

An initial column pass visits every sampled actor within its shared budget,
retaining each actor's first disclosure instead of stopping after the first
viewer. Later unrelated prefixes cannot replace an already observed zero-step
exposure for another sampled viewer.

Unprojected compiler-typed Row/Page payloads also witness column reads when the
returned field equals that committed physical value and its field-visibility
gate admits this viewer. This covers real media ids without manufacturing an
alternate asset ledger. Projected scalar/shape values retain the paired-world
requirement; a coincidentally matching constant is not column provenance. A
private field's redacted null is never treated as disclosure of a null value.

Absent contrary independent intent, caller-chosen role advice requires new
unconditional table authority; an additional trusted resource/ownership grant
is a scoped delegation boundary requiring its own reduction. Fixed enrollment
keeps the narrower read/delete/roster comparison. This avoids treating ordinary
self-enrollment for one's own records as privileged provisioning.

Principal-only global membership grants also participate in advice. An
unqualified viewer probe of a required unique principal denotes current
membership; authored deletion/principal reassignment can revoke it. Membership
creation receives escalation advice when it contradicts a pure caller-based
independent provisioning invariant (table insert/update/write or named operation).
Principal reassignment uses update authority including private-field ownership;
upsert creation and reassignment are judged on their separate arms. A dominating
global appointer role may reside in a separate roster, with all its qualifiers
retained when checking implication.
Ordinary account enrollment without such provisioning intent is not guessed to
be an administrator grant. Scoped/qualified membership still needs its reduction.

Paired query observations tag successful data separately from authored logical
refusals, retaining refusal codes and messages. Only engine-provided authored
rule/constraint markers qualify; incomplete reads, resource exhaustion and
ambiguous engine errors remain outside logical comparison. This prevents both
missed refusal oracles and collisions between a payload shape and a refusal.
Synthetic principal sampling separates the focal row's attribution from the
supporting roster/assignment recipient; finite enumeration still never proves
that all tuples are covered.

**NOT READY** for committed revision `63dfa5e6c5a0cc425b31fb0d1312eccd7a4312d6`.

1. **Pending rows are assigned the wrong write arm.** In [histories.rs:179](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/histories.rs:179), predecessor matching uses `live`, which excludes rows before their `not before` time. The engine permits updating those physical, unexpired rows. Consequently, an authorized update can become a reachable **insert** counterexample and fail check/test against insert-only intent. The delete loop similarly skips actual deletions of pending rows.

   **Repair:** determine predecessors and executed arms using the engine’s write-target semantics; keep read visibility separate. This is required by §12.2’s correct-arm contract.

2. **Generated column pairs lack the required committed-world equality check.** In [column_reads.rs:83](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b17-sec3/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/security/challenges/column_reads.rs:83), non-protected-field comparison runs only for fixture alternates. Generated worlds rebuild captures from authored inputs, so changing the protected column can also change recorded attribution and its downstream aggregates. Repeatable output differences then need not isolate that column, contrary to §12.8. Additionally, line 103 records `other`, the pre-validation candidate, as the alternate world—even when maintenance changed its committed contents.

   **Repair:** compare both committed readbacks for every column pair, requiring identical non-protected base fields, captures, identities and populations. Decline mismatches as unsupported, and serialize the actual committed worlds.

These are correctness repairs to witness construction and attribution; they do not require proving unsupported reductions. Finite campaigns must continue to leave completeness unproved.

Source/design review only; no tests run or edits made.
